annāḷil, uḻaippataṟkēṟṟa uṭalnalamum piṟa nalaṅkaḷum  iṉṟi vaṟumaiyuṟṟu varuntum iravalar palar iruntaṉar. avaruṭ kaṟṟōrum kuṭippiṟantōrum eṉap palatiṟattar uṇṭu. avariṭaiyē pukunta vaṟumai peruṅkēṭṭiṉaic ceytatu; kuṭip piṟantāriṭattē iḷivanta coṟkaḷaip pēcattakka kuṟṟattait tōṟṟuvittatu; kaṟṟōr nalla poruḷkaḷai naṉkeṭuttuc coṉṉālum, vaṟumaiyāl avar coṟkaḷ cellāk kācu kaḷāyiṉa. avarkku vēṇṭuvaṉa nalkum celvarai nōkkiya kālai, avariṭaiyē īyātu karappōrum, īttal kaṭaṉeṉa vuṇarntu nalkuvōrum iruppatu tiruvaḷḷuvarkkut terintatu. avarai oppanōkkiyapōtu celvar cilarum vaṟiyar palarumāka iruntaṉar innilai vaḷarumāyiṉ, muṭivil celva vāḻvaic cīraḻittaṟkēṟṟa tīya muyaṟcikaḷ vaṟiyavar kūṭṭattē piṟakkum; iṟutiyil akkūṭṭattiṉ muyaṟci veṟṟi eytavum kūṭum; annilai eytāvakaiyil celvarai nōkki, kaṟṟōrum kuṭip piṟantōrum vaṟumaiyuṟiṉ tammiyalpukku māṟāṉavaṟṟaic ceyvareṉa vaṟpuṟuttiṉār. irakkum iravalar poruṭṭu varunti, “karavātīyum celvariṭattē ceṉṟu irattal vēṇṭum; avarpāl irappatum ītalpōl iṉpam taruvatām; irappāraik kaṇṭu ikaḻntu eḷḷātu īpavarkaḷaik kāṇpatē pēriṉpam” eṉat terivittār. irappārk kīyattakka celvamuṭaiyārai nōkki, “irappavar illārāyiṉ ivvulakiyalil nam iyakkam marappāvai iyakkampōla irukkum; ivvulakil nam tōṟṟat tāl oru payaṉum illaiyām” eṉṟu aṟivuṟuttiṉār. piṉpu irattalum ītalumākiya ceykaikaḷ perumpālum makkaḷiṭaiyē maṉappiḷavai vaḷarppatēyaṉṟi ataṉaik kuṟaikkappayaṉpaṭuva tāka illai. itaṉait teḷiyak kaṇṭa tiruvaḷḷuvar, īkaikkum irattalē kāraṇamātalāl, ataṉait tavirppatu takkateṉa eṇṇiṉār.
iravalar kuḻuviṭaicceṉṟu avar paṭum tuṉpattaik kaṇṭār. avar ivvaṟumait tuṉpattai irantu tīrkkum maṉavaṉmaiyuṭaiyarāka iruntaṉar. māṉamiṉṟi oruvar muṉṉiṉṟu irattalai eṇṇiyatum tiruvaḷḷuvar uḷḷam uruki melintatu. āyiṉum, avar ataṉaicceyyum iravalar uḷḷattiṉ vaṉmaiyai yuṇarntār. avvaṉmai muṟuki yeḻumāyiṉ ettuṇai vallaracum taviṭupoṭi yākumē eṉa eṇṇi yañciṉār. iravalar muṉṉiṉṟu, irakkak karutiniṟkum avar uḷḷattai māṟṟa niṉaintu “āviṟkuc ciṟitu nīr vēṇṭum eṉa oruvarai irantālum, nāvukku ac collāl piṟakkum iḻivu peritāyiṟṟē” eṉṟum, “oruvaṉ tāṉ muyaṉṟu peṟṟatu koṇṭu uṇṇum uṇaviṉum uyarnta vuṇavu kiṭaiyātē” eṉṟum eṭuttōtiṉār. iravalaruṭ cilar, karavātu īyum kaivaṇmaiyuṭaiyavarai irantu vāḻalāmē eṉak karutuva taṟinta vaḷḷuvar, avariṭattum iravāmai kōṭi nalaṅkaḷaic ceyyumē eṉṟār. avariṭaiyē, uḻaippavar uḻaippiṉ payaṉai uṟiñciyuṇṭu peruñcuvai nukarntu tirinta kūṭṭaṅkaḷum iruntaṉa. ak kūṭṭattavaruṭ cilar, emmai irantu vāḻavēṇṭu meṉṟē ulakai iyaṟṟiya mutalvaṉ paṭaittuḷḷāṉ;  eṅkaḷukkeṉa varaiyaṟukkappaṭṭa toḻilkaḷuḷ irattalum oṉṟāka aṟa nūlkaḷ kuṟittuḷḷaṉa eṉṟu niṉain toḻukiṉar. atu muṟṟilum poy. ulakiyaṟṟiya mutalvaṉ cilar irantum uyir vāḻtal vēṇṭumeṉakkaruti avarkaḷaip paṭaittiruppāṉāyiṉ, aḵtu uyir vāḻkkaiyaik keṭuttaṟkuc ceyta tīya cūḻcciyākaliṉ, attīviṉaiyāl avaṉ keṭṭaḻi vāṉāka eṉat tam maṭitta vāyait tiṟantu veṭitta coṟkaḷai viḷampiṉār.
iṉi, ivariṉum kaṭaippaṭṭa kīḻmakkaḷ palar potuvāka makkaḷiṭaiyē iruntaṉar celvar, vaṟiyar, kaṟṟavar, kallātavar, mutiyar, iḷaiyar, aracar, aṭiyar eṉṟa ellāvakaiyāṉa makkaḷilum ik kīḻ makkaḷ iruntaṉar. celvak kālam allaṟ kālameṉṟa ellāk kālattilum avarkaḷ neñcil avalam ciṟitum iṉṟiyiruntaṉar. etaṉaiyum tam viruppappaṭiyē ceyva rēyaṉṟip piṟar kūṟuvatu kēṭka viḻaiyār. accamē avarkaṭku oḻukkamākum. eññāṉṟum piṟaraik kuṟṟaṅ
kūṟuvatum piṟar maṟai (irakaciyam) yaṟintu tūṟṟuvatum avaratu ciṟappiyalpu. avarkaḷ tam ecciṟkaiyāl īyōṭṭamāṭṭārkaḷ. kaṉṉattil aṟaintu pallaiyutirkkum kaṇṇaṟaiyarkku vēṇṭuvaṉa viraintu vaḻaṅkuvar. cāṉṟōr oṉṟu collap payaṉpaṭuvar; ik kayavarkaḷ kollap payaṉpaṭuvar. piṟar uṭukkum uṭaiyilum uṇṇum uṇavilum kuṟukuṟutta pārvai celuttik kuṟṟam kāṇpatil peru viruppu ṭaiyar. ivarkaḷaik kaṇṭa tiruvaḷḷuvar “eṟṟiṟkuriyar kayavar!” eṉa eṇṇiṉār. muṭivil ivarkaḷ “oṉṟu uṟṟakkāl viraintu tammaiyē viṟṟaṟkuriyar” eṉṟu tēṟi, ivarpōl “oppāriyām kaṇṭatillai” eṉṟu kūṟikkoṇṭu ceṉṟār.
tuṟavu vāḻvu
ivvāṟu maṉaivāḻvu, araciyal vāḻvu, poruḷ vāḻvu mutaliya palavakai vāḻvuvakaikaḷaik kaṇṭu uṇmaiyaṟivu perukivanta tiruvaḷḷuvarkku vayatum mutirntu varuvatāyiṟṟu. makkaḷuṭaiya vāḻvu tāḻvu kaḷaiyum, iṉpat
tuṉpaṅkaḷaiyum aṟivu aṟiyāmai kaḷaiyum, veṟṟi tōlvi
kaḷaiyum nēriṟkaṇṭu avvak kālaṅkaḷilum makkaḷuṭaiya uḷḷam, urai , ceyal eṉpaṉa iyaṉṟa muṟaiyai avar naṉkaṟintār. avaruṭaiya naṉ makkaḷum aṟivu nirampit tattam vāḻkkaikaḷai naṭātta luṟṟaṉar. maṉaivimakkaḷ pāl tōṉṟiya aṉpu, cuṟṟattār, tuṇaivar, naṇpar mutalāyiṉārpāl toṭarntu aruḷāki evvuyir pālum paṭaruvatāyiṟṟu. āṇṭu mutira mutira aṉpu mutirntu aruḷākiya kaṉiyai viḷaivippatu vāḻviṉ kuṟippātal viḷaṅkiṟṟu. anta aruḷ uyarntōr pāl peruñ celvamāyt tikaḻntatu. ataṉaiyuṭaiyār viṉaiyaccamiṉṟi iṉpamuṟṟaṉar. avarkku allalē uṇṭākavillai. aruḷuṭaiyār aruṇeṟiyiṉ viḻuviṉāl mīḷat tēṟit teḷiyum vaḻiyiṉṟi varuntu vataiyum tiruvaḷḷuvar kaṇṭār. “poruḷaṟṟār pūppar orukāl; aruḷaṟṟār aṟṟār” eṉat terintār. piṉpu, aruḷāḷar pulāl uṇṇātu vāḻakkaṇṭār. pulāl uṇavu oruvaṉ taṉ ūṉ perukkutaṟkup payaṉpaṭṭatu. ataṉāl, ‘taṉṉūṉ perukkaṟkut tāṉ piṟitu ūṉ uṇpāṉ eṅṅaṉam aruḷāḷuvaṉ?” eṉa ārāyntu, ūṉ tiṉpavarkku aruḷāṭciyillaiyeṉat teḷi vuṟṟār. ūṉuṇṇāmaiyāl uyirkaḷ tām niṟṟaṟkuriya uṭam pōṭu niṟkamuṭiyum; makkaḷuyir piṟa makkaḷuyiriṉ uṭampait tiṉṉaluṟiṉ, makkaṭ kūṭṭam immānilattil arukik keṭum eṉpatu kaṇṭu, “uṇṇāmai yuḷḷatu uyirnilai” yeṉṟār. akkālattē uyirkkolai puriyum vēḷvikaḷ naṭaipeṟṟu vantaṉa. āyiram vēḷvipurintōṉ viṇṇulaka vēntāyt tēvarum makkaḷum kaikūppit toḻu mēṉmaiyuṟuvaṉ eṉṟa karuttum avvēḷviyāḷariṭai muṟpaṭṭu niṉṟatu. ataṉāl tiruvaḷḷuvar “avicorintu āyiram vēṭṭaliṉ oṉṟaṉ uyir cekuttu uṇṇāmai naṉṟu” eṉavum, “kollāṉ pulālai maṟuttāṉaik kaikūppi, ellā uyirum toḻum” eṉavum vaṟpuṟutti vantār. ivvāṟē uyirkaḷaik koṉṟu vāḻum vāḻkkaiyum ciṟanta vāḻkkaiyaṉṟu eṉat teḷivippārāy. “aṟaviṉai yāteṉil kollāmai” eṉavum, “nallā ṟeṉap paṭuvatu yāteṉiṉ yātoṉṟum, kollāmai cūḻum neṟi” yeṉavum eṭuttōtiṉar.
innilaiyil tiruvaḷḷuvaruṭaiya uḷḷam tavattai nōkkat toṭaṅkiṟṟu. tavamuṭaiyār, pakai keṭuttalum naṇparai eṭuttalumākiya ceyalkaḷai eḷitil ceytaṉar; avar vēṇṭiya vaṟṟai vēṇṭiyavāṟē peṟṟu iṉpuṟṟaṉar. tavamāvatu yāteṉa ārāyntār. tattamakkuriya kaṭamai kaḷait tavaṟātu ceypavar vēṇṭiya vēṇṭiyāṅku eytuvatu kaṇṭu, “tavam ceyvār taṅkarumam ceyvār” eṉṟār. tavamuṭaiyārai aṟitaṟku vaḻiyaṟiya muyaṉṟa tiruvaḷḷuvar, uṟṟanōy poṟuttalum, piṟa uyirkaṭku uṟukaṇ ceyyāmaiyum tavamuṭaiyārpāl uruvāy viḷaṅkak kaṇṭār. tuṉpam cuṭaccuṭat tākkiya vaḻi, tavamuṭaiyār tam karumattil uṟaip
puṟṟu niṉṟu aṟivoḷi peṟṟu mēṉmai yuṟṟaṉar. tavamuṭai
yār uḷḷam aruḷ niṟaintiruntataṉāl piṟa uyirkaṭku nalam ceyvatē tavaviṉaiyākak karutiṉar. uṟṟanōy poṟuttalāl taṉṉalam pēṇāmaiyum, uyirkkuṟu kaṇ ceyyā maiyāl piṟarkkeṉa muyaluvatum tavamāyiṉa. ivaṟṟaic ceytaṟ kaṇ maṉaivāḻvu taṭaiceytu vantatu. mikac cilarē ittavattai mēṟkoṇṭaṉar. itaṟkuk kāraṇam kāṇaluṟṟa tiruvaḷḷuvar, ittavam karuti maṉaivāḻvait tuṟanta periyōrkku vēṇṭum tuppuravu nalkutaṟkāka maṟṟaiyar tavattai mēṟkoḷḷa maṟantaṉar pōlum eṉṟu koṇṭār.
iṅṅaṉam, tavam kuṟittu maṉaivāḻkkaiyait tuṟantu ceṉṟa cāṉṟōriṭaiyē pōlit tuṟavikaḷum uḷarāyiṉar avaruṭcilar maṉaimakkaḷaik kākkum valiyiṉṟit tuṟantār vēṭantāṅkiṉar. cilar makkaḷ aṟiyāmaiyaik karuviyākak koṇṭu poruḷum iṉpamum nukarak karutit tuṟavuruk
koṇṭaṉar vēṟu cilar tām ceyta peruṅ kuṟṟattai maṟaippatu kuṟittut tuṟavu nāṭakam naṭittaṉar. avaratu vaṉkaṇmai kaṇṭu añciya nam tiruvaḷḷuvar, avaruṭ cilaruṭaiya vañcaṉai veḷippaṭa avareytiya tuṉpam kaṇṭu, avarkaḷai nōkki, “paṟṟaṟṟēm eṉpār paṭiṟṟoḻukkam eṟṟeṟṟeṉṟu, ētam palavum tarum” eṉṟu kūṟiṉar. ēṉai
makkaḷaip pārttu, maṉattil mācu taṅka, māperun tuṟavipōlum vēṭa muṭaiyār  palaruḷar; avarkaḷai ārāyntu aṟital vēṇṭu meṉa aṟivuṟuttiṉār.
it tuṟavuvēṭam piṟar poruḷaik kaḷavu koḷ vataṟkuc cilarkkut tuṇaiyāyiṟṟu. kaḷaviṉāl ākiya celvam āvatu pōlat tōṉṟi muṭivil aḷaviṟantu keṭuvatum, kaḷvar tam kaḷḷam veḷippaṭṭavaḻip perun tuṉpam eytuvatum tiruvaḷḷuvar kaṇṭār. uṇmait tuṟavikaḷai nōkkiṉār. avar uḷḷattē kaḷavillai; poymmaiyum illai. uḷḷattālum poyyā toḻukum avarkaḷai ulakattār uḷḷattil vaittup pārāṭṭip paraviṉar. puṟat tūymai nīrāl amaivatupōla akattūymai avar vaḻaṅkiya vāymaiyāṟ kāṇappaṭṭatu. vāymaiyāvatu  yāteṉa nūlkaḷai ārāynta vaḷḷuvar, “vāymaiyeṉap paṭuvatu yāteṉiṉ yātoṉṟum tīmaiyilāta colal” eṉṟum, “poymmaiyum vāymai yiṭattapuraitīrnta naṉmai payakkum eṉiṉ” eṉṟum teḷintār. piṟarkkum, “yām meyyāk kaṇṭavaṟṟuḷ illai eṉait
toṉṟum vāymaiyiṉalla piṟa” eṉṟu eṭutturaittār.
taṉṉalam pēṇātu piṟarkkeṉa muyalum peṟṟi yarākiya tuṟavikaḷ uṭal mutirntu valikuṉṟiyirutta lāl, eḷitil avarkaḷ vekuḷikku iraiyāyiṉar. ataṉāl tīmaikaḷ pala uṇṭāyiṉa. cellumiṭam cellā viṭaṅkaḷait tērātu, palar ciṉamkoṇṭu iraṇṭiṭattum tuṉpamē uṟṟaṉar. atukaṇṭa tiruvaḷḷuvar ciṉattāl nakaiyum uvakaiyum aḻivatu nōkkiṉār. avaṟṟiṟkuc ciṉam pakaiyāvatum aṟintār. ciṉaṅkoṇṭavartāmē yaṉṟi avar iṉattārum ataṉāl tuṉpuṟṟaṉar. ciṉam koḷḷātāṉ tāṉ karutiyavellām karutiyavāṟu peṟṟu iṉpuṟu talaikkaṇṭu, ciṉaṅkoḷvārkkuk kāṭṭiṉār icciṉattāl, palar piṟarkkut tam collālum ceyalālum iṉṉāmai ceytaṉar. atu kaṇṭu varuntiya tiruvaḷḷuvar, it tuṟavikaḷatu aṟivupperumaiyai eṇṇiṉār. atu payaṉpaṭāmai kaṇṭu parintār. 
“aṟiviṉāṉ ākuva tuṇṭō piṟitiṉnōy taṉnōy pōṟ pōṟṟāk kaṭai” yeṉṟu aṟivuṟuttiṉār. cilar, iṉṉā
ceytārai vāḷātuviṭiṉ mēlum ataṉaiyē ceyvarē yeṉṟaṉar. avarkaṭkut tiruvaḷḷuvar,  “iṉṉā ceytārai yoṟuttal avarnāṇa naṉṉayam ceytuviṭal” eṉṟu teruṭṭiṉār.
potuvāka ulakaraiyum ciṟappākat tuṟaviyaraiyum kaṇṭu avaravar uḷḷam, urai, ceyal vakaikaḷai aṟintu pōnta tiruvaḷḷuvarkku uṇmait tuṟaviṉkaṇ karuttuc ceṉṟatu. celvam, uṭal, iḷamai mutaliyavaṟṟiṉ nilaiyāmai pulaṉāyiṟṟu. vāḻum vāḻvil ovvoru poḻutum uṭal vāḻvaik kuṟaittukkoṇṭē varuvatai naṉku eṇṇiṉār. uyirkkum uṭampukkum uḷḷa toṭarpu, oru paṟavaikkum ataṉ kuṭampaikkum uḷḷa toṭarpāka iruttalait teḷintār. uṭaliṉ nīṅkiya uyir eṅkē pōkum eṉa niṉaittār. uṭampukkuṭ pōntu otuṅki yiruntu viṭṭup piṉṉar nīṅki yēkum uyirkkup pukumiṭam oṉṟu iruntē tīra vēṇṭumeṉat teḷintār. ivvuṇarvu mutiramutira uṭampiṉkaṇ uḷḷa paṟṟuk kuṟaiyat talaippaṭṭatu. uṭampiṭamāka iruntu paṟṟiṉai viḷaivikkum aimpulaṉkaḷai yaṭakkiṉār. aṭakkātu akappaṟṟiṉaippaṟṟi niṟpārai iṭumpaikaṉ paṟṟik koṇṭu viṭātiruppatai aṟintār paṟṟiṉṟit tuṟantu nōṟkum periyōrkaḷai nōkkiṉār. avarpāl oṉṟiṉpālum paṟṟi llāmai iyalpāy amaintukiṭantatu.
paṟṟu ēṉ uṇṭākiṟateṉpatu kāṇaluṟṟār, paṟṟu piṟappukkuk kāraṇamātalaik kaṇṭār. piṟap piṟappukkaḷ uḷavātaṟkuriya kāraṇattai aṟiyaluṟṟār. uyirkku uṭampōṭu uṇṭākiya toṭarpukkuk kāraṇam pulaṉāyiṟṟu. eṉṉai kāraṇam ? uṭampōṭu kūṭiyuṟaiyum uyirkku noṭitōṟum aṟivu viḷakkam uṇṭākiṟatu; uṭampu aṟivu viḷakkattukkut tuṇaiyā vatāl., uyir aṟivu viḷakkam peṟātirunta nilaiyiruntateṉavum, ataṉaip peṟum poruṭṭu vanta uṭampu uyirāl tēṭik
koḷḷap peṟāmaiyāl iraṇṭaiyum toṭarpupaṭuttu
vōṉ iṟaivaṉ eṉṟum, uyirum uṭampum oṉṟai yoṉṟu iṉṟiyamaiyā tiruppataṟkē ip paṟṟu uṇṭākiṟateṉṟum, ip paṟṟum aṟivu viḷakkamē ceykiṟateṉṟum, uyir aṟivu muḻuviḷakkam peṟṟa vaḻi uṭampiṉ toṭarpu vēṇṭā teṉṟum, iṟaivaṉ viḷaṅkiya aṟivē vuruvāṉavaṉ eṉṟum, ataṉāl avaṟku uṭampu vēṇṭā maiyiṉ avaṉpaṟṟaṟṟavaṉ eṉṟum, avaṉ uṇmai yuṇarntu avaṉpāl paṟṟuc ceyyiṉ aṟiviṉ muḻuviḷakkamum ataṉāl uṭampiṉ toṭarpākiya piṟappu uṇṭākā teṉṟum teḷintār. piṟarkkum teḷiya vuṇartta niṉaintu, “paṟṟuka paṟṟaṟṟāṉ paṟṟiṉai” eṉṟum, “appaṟṟiṉaip paṟṟuka paṟṟu viṭaṟku” eṉṟum aṟivuṟuttiṉār. tuṟavu neṟiyum avarukkuk kaikūṭuva tāyiṟṟu.
tuṟaviṉkaṇ niṉṟa tiruvaḷḷuvar, paṟṟaṟṟā ṉākiya meyp poruḷai yuṇarattoṭaṅkiṉār. epporuḷ ettaṉmait tāyiṉum apporuḷiṉ meymmait toṭarpu kāṇpatē aṟiveṉak kaṇṭār. kaṇṭavaḻi, uṭampiṉ vēṟāka uyir viḷaṅkuvatu tērntu, avvuyir uṭampiṟkuḷ iyaṅkum maṉa mutaliyavaṟṟālākiya nuṇṇuṭampiṉ uḷḷē viḷaṅkuvatu aṟintār. uḷḷē iruppatupaṟṟi uyirkku  uḷḷam eṉap peyariṭṭār avvuḷḷam taṉṉiṉ vēṟāka vuḷḷaṉavellām nilaipēṟiṉṟi aḻivatāl nilaiyāka vuḷḷatākiya cemporuḷ uṇmaiyiṉaik kaṇṭu taṉ aṟivu naṉku viḷaṅkappeṟum; ataṉāl uṭampai vēṇṭātoḻiyum eṉṟu uṇarntu, “ōrttu uḷḷam uḷḷatu uṇariṉ, oru talaiyāp pērttuḷḷa vēṇṭā piṟappu” eṉṟār. itaṉai naṉkaṟinta piṟkālattē tōṉṟiya meykaṇṭatēvarum uyirai “uḷḷam” eṉṟē vaḻaṅkiṉār.
cemporuḷ uṇmaiyai yaṟiyum aṟivu, ēṉai uṭampu mutaliya cārpukaḷai nōkkac ciṟanta cārpāka iruppatait tiruvaḷḷuvar ciṟappuṟat teḷintu, uṭampum ulakumākiya cārpukaḷ tamakku uṇṭākā vaṇṇam ac cemporuḷ aṟivē cārpāka oḻukiṉār. uṭampōṭu kūṭiyiruntē itaṉaic ceytu ciṟantamai yāṉ, poruṭcārpukaḷākiya uṭampulakukaḷāl uṇṭākum nōykaḷ tammaic cārā toḻivatu kaṇṭār. “cārpuṇarntu cārpukeṭa voḻukiṉ maṟṟaḻittuc cārtārā cārtarum nōy” eṉṟu aṟivuṟuttiṉār.
uṭampōṭu uḷḷavaraiyum nukarvaṉa nukarntu vāḻa vēṇṭi yiruttalāl, cemporuḷa ṟivupeṟṟu ulakiyaṟ cārpu keṭa oḻukuvatu eṅṅaṉamākum eṉṟoru viṉā avar uḷḷattē eḻuntatu. ataṟku viṭaiyāka, itukāṟum uṭampōṭu peṟṟirunta toṭarpāl uyirkku uṇṭākiya avā ataṉait tāṅki
niṟkum uṭampu vaḻiniṉṟu, aṟivai maṟaittup piṟappai yuṇṭu
paṇṇu meṉṟum, aḵtuṇṭā kātavāṟu ataṉai aṟut
toḻukavēṇṭum eṉṟum kaṇṭār. ataṉai aṟuttaṟkuriya eḷiyavaḻi, iṟaivaṉai vāḻtti vaṇaṅkuvatē eṉat tērntār.
ivvāṟu meyyuṇarntu avā vaṟuttoḻukuṅ kālattil it tirukkuṟaḷai eḻutaluṟṟār. ataṉāl mutaṟkaṇ kaṭavuḷai vāḻttum vāḻttiṉaik kūṟiṉār. ataṉkaṇ tāmuṇarnta iṟaivaṉai, ātipakavaṉ, vālaṟivaṉ, malarmicaiyēkiṉāṉ, vēṇṭutal vēṇṭāmai yillātavaṉ, iṟaivaṉ, poṟi
vāyil aintavittāṉ, taṉakku uvamaiyillātāṉ, aṟavāḻi antaṇaṉ, eṇkuṇattāṉ eṉṟu viḷakkiṉār. avaṉpāl paṟṟukkoṇṭu, ēṉai uṭampu ulakavāḻvukaḷil paṟṟuvai
yātu vaḻi paṭṭoḻu kiṉāl, uṭampōṭu kūṭiyuṟaiyum vāḻvu iṉitu iyalum eṉavum, ārāta iyalpiṉaiyuṭaiya avāvāl uṇṭākum tuṉpaṅkaḷ illaiyām eṉavum, iruviṉaiyum vantu aṇaiyā eṉavum, maṉattiṉkaṇ kavalai mutaliyaṉa illā toḻiyum eṉavum, piṟavit toṭarpu illātu keṭum eṉavum kūṟiṉār.
ivvāṟu vāḻvōr ēṉaiyuyirkaḷ iṉpuṟṟu vāḻvataiyē virumpi ataṟkēṟṟa paṇiyiṉaic ceyvar. ataṟku avvuyirkaḷāl niṉaittapaṭiyē peṟṟukkoḷḷa muṭiyātatu maḻaiyātalāl, piṉpu maḻaiyaik kūṟiṉār. ivaṟṟaiyellām ārāyntu kaṇṭuraikkum āṟṟa luṭaiyār tuṟavuneṟi niṉṟu nītta perumai yuṭaiyōr. avar perumaiyai maḻai
yiṉaiyaṭuttuk kūṟi aṟattaip potuvakaiyil eṭuttōti valiyuṟuttaruḷiṉār.
ūḻ
aṟamporuḷ iṉpam eṉṟa muttiṟattu vāḻvilum cila nikaḻccikaḷ kāraṇam teriyāmalē uḷavātalait tiruvaḷḷuvar kaṇṭār. oṉṟai niṉaittuc ceyiṉ, oṉṟēyaṉṟi vēṟoṉṟu viḷaivatum, avvoṉṟē viḷaiyā toḻiyātoḻivatum, muṟṟilum niṉaiyāta toṉṟu vantu nērppaṭuvatum piṟavum tiruvaḷḷuvarkku muṉpirunta cāṉṟōrkkup peruṅ kalakkattai viḷaivittaṉa. viṉai, ceyvatu, ceyappaṭu poruḷ,iṭam, kālam, karuvi, nōkkam, payaṉ eṉa eṇvakaiyāṉa kāraṇaṅkaḷai oru viṉai nikaḻccikku ētuvākat tolkāppiyar mutaliya cāṉṟōr kaṇṭiruntaṉar. mēlē kūṟiya nikaḻccikaḷ ivvārāyc ciyāl viḷakkam peṟātoḻintaṉa. avai kāraṇam pulappaṭā vakaiyil nikaḻntuvarakkaṇṭa paṇṭaic cāṉṟōr, avaṟṟiṉ payaṉai nukarntu kaḻittaṉarāyiṉum, pulappaṭāta akkāraṇattai yaṟiyum muyaṟciyil iṭaiviṭātu uḻaittu vantaṉar.
vāḻvil oruvaṉ nukarum iṉpamum tuṉpamum avaṉ ceyyum viṉai vaḻiyāka uḷavāvaṉa eṉpatu iyalpākat terinta oṉṟu kāraṇam pulappaṭātu nikaḻnta nikaḻcci kaḷālum iṉpamum tuṉpamumē payaṉāy viḷaintaṉa. viṉaippayaṉākat tōṉṟum iṉpat tuṉpaṅkaḷē, kāraṇam pulappaṭāta nikaḻcci
kaḷiṉ viḷaivāyiṉamaiyiṉ, iṉpat tuṉpak kāraṇamāṉa viṉaiyē, kāraṇam pulappaṭāta vakaiyil nikaḻum viḷaivu kaṭkum kāraṇamām eṉṟu kaṇṭaṉar. ‘piṟappokkum ellā vuyirkku meṉiṉum, oruvar celvattiṟ piṟantu iṉpuṟutalum, oruvar vaṟumaiyiṟ piṟantu tuṉpuṟutalum kāraṇam pulappaṭāta vakaiyil nikaḻum nikaḻccikaḷākum; veyiliṟ cellum iruvaruḷ oruvar aḻaṟṟeṟippu (sun- stroke) uṟṟu iṟappatum maṟṟavar iruppatum maḻai nāḷil oru maṉaiyiṉ kaṇ irukkum palaruḷ oruvar iṭiyēṟuṇṭu iṟappatum, piṟar iruppatum kāraṇam aṟiyavārāta nikaḻcci kaḷākum. ivvāṟē, paṇṭai nāḷil intiyāvukku vaḻi kāṇpāṉ muyaṉṟa kolampas amerikkāvaik kaṇṭatum, in nāḷil rēṭiyam kāṇa muyaṉṟavar pōlōṉiyam kaṇṭatum mēṟkūṟiya nikaḻcciyiṉ pāṟpaṭṭaṉavām. innikaḻcci kaṭkum, ivaṟṟiṉ payaṉai uṟṟōrkkum celavum muyaṟciyum mutaliya viṉai vakaiyāl toṭarpuṇṭātaliṉ, pulappaṭāta kāraṇattai viṉai yeṉṟē paṇṭaiyōr kūṟiṉar.
oruviṉaiyiṉ payaṉākiya iṉpamum tuṉpamum avviṉai muṭivilē perumpālum uḷavākum; cila iṭaiyil iṉpam payantu muṭivil vēṟoṉṟu viḷaivikkum; cila toṭakkattil tuṉpam payantu muṭivil iṉpam payakkum. ivvāṟṟāl viṉai vakaikaṭkēṟpa viṉaip payaṉkaḷ mutal iṭai muṭivu eṉṟa viṭaṅkaḷil tōṉṟuvataik kaṇṭu, kāraṇa kāriyaṅkaḷai ārāynta aṟiñar avai oru muṟaipaṟṟi nikaḻvatu kaṇṭaṉar.
am muṟaiyiṉai ārāynta aṟiñar, viṉaip payaṉkaḷākiya iṉpa tuṉpaṅkaḷ, viṉaiyiṉkaṇ aruvāyiruntu, avviṉai, viṉai mutal, karuvi, kālam eṉpaṉavaṟṟāl toḻiṟ paṭuṅkāl, tāmum mukiḻttu arumpip pūttuc ciṟappikkum malarpōlciṟantu viṉai muṭiviṉ kaṇ naṟumalar payaṉpaṭuvatu kaṇṭuṇarntaṉar. pūttu maṇañcey ceyalai ūḻttal eṉpatu tamiḻ vaḻakku. “iṇarūḻttum nāṟā malaraṉaiyar” eṉat tiruvaḷḷuvarum, pūttu maṇaṅkamaḻac camainta arumpiṉai “ūḻmukai” (naṟ. 115) eṉavum, maṇaṅkamaḻntu vayaṅkum pūvai “ūḻuṟupū” (naṟ. 326) eṉavum cāṉṟōrum vaḻaṅkuvatu kāṇka. ivvūḻmukaiyum ūḻuṟu pūvumpōla, iṉpamum tuṉpamumākiya payaṉkaḷaimaṭṭil payakkum viṉaiyum viṉai mutalum karuviyum piṟavumākiya, kāraṇam viḷaṅkāta viṉaikaḷai, ūḻviṉaiyeṉac cāṉṟōr vaḻaṅkiṉar.
oru viṉaikkuriya payaṉ, ataṉ viṉaimutalōṭu toṭarpum urimaiyum peṟṟu viḷaṅkuvatu kaṇkūṭu kāraṇam pulappaṭāta viṉaippayaṉkaḷ oruvarai yaṭaiyumāyiṉ, avarukkum, ap payaṉkaṭkuk kāraṇamāṉa viṉaikkum toṭarpuṇṭeṉpatu teḷivām. payaṉai nōkka, viṉai kālattāl muṟpaṭuvatāl, kāraṇam pulappaṭātu nikaḻum viṉaippayaṉkaḷ oruvaruṭaiya muṉṉai viṉaippayaṉ eṉavum vaḻaṅkappaṭṭaṉa. ivvāṟu, viṉaiyuṇarvu tōṉṟik kaṟṟār kallātār eṉṟa irutiṟat tāriṭaiyilum naṉku paraviyirunta kālam caṅkakālam. “yām
cey tolviṉaik kevaṉ pētuṟṟaṉai”yeṉap pēcum nallantuvaṉār mutaliya cāṉṟōr irunta kālam accaṅka
kālam. atuvē tiruvaḷḷuvar kālamumātaliṉ, ivviṉaiyaip paṟṟiyum tiruvaḷḷuvar tamatu nūlil kūṟum karuttuṭaiyarāṉār aṟam viṉaiyiṉmēlum, ēṉaip poruḷum iṉpamum viṉaippayaṉ mēlum niṟkak kaṇṭu, aṟattukkum poruṭkum iṭaiyē ūḻviṉai yeṉap peyariṭṭu, itaṉaippaṟṟit tiruvaḷḷuvar pattut tirukkuṟaḷkaḷ vaḻaṅkiṉār.
maṭimaiyē vaṭivākavuḷḷa oruvaṉ, oru kāraṇamum paṟṟātu muyaṟciyuṭaiyaṉāy oru viṉai ceytu iṉpuṟuvatum, muyaṟci yuruvāya oruvaṉ etirpārā vakaiyil maṭiyuṟṟut tuṉpuṟuvatum tiruvaḷḷuvar kaṇṭār; ciṟanta aṟiñaṉoruvaṉ orōvaḻi aṟivu maḻuṅkiyiruntāṉ; piṟitorukāl oruvaṉ piṟa aṟiñar kāṇṭaṟkariya nuṭpattai yuṇarntāṉ; ciṟanta celvamuṭai yārpāl iruttaṟkuriya kūrtta aṟivu, vaṟumait tīyil vataṅku vōṉpāl oḷiviṭṭut tikaḻn tatu; oruvaṉ celvam ceytaṟku ivai nallaṉa eṉat tērntu avaṟṟaic ceytāṉāka, avai tīyavāyc celvakkēṭṭiṟkuḷḷāyiṉa; piṟaṉoruvaṉ uṇar viṉṟiyē tīcceyal ceyya, atu mikka celvam payantu avaṟkuc ciṟappai viḷaivittatu. miṭiyāl varuntu moruvaṉ miṭimaikku ētuvākiya kuṇañ ceyalkaḷaiyum iṭam poruḷ kaḷaiyum vilakka muyaṉṟāṉ; avaṉ muyaṟci veṟṟitarā toḻintatu. peruñ celva muṭaiyavaroruvar nukarakkaṭava nukarporuḷ muṟṟavum nirampap
peṟṟuṇṇum pīṭuṭaiyarāyiruntum, nukarātu varuntak kaṇṭār. ivarkaḷ ceyalkaḷellāvaṟṟiṟkum ūḻviṉaiyē kāraṇamāvatu uṇarntu nammaṉōraṟiya eṭutturaittār. viṉai yuṇarvuṭaiyārai nōkki, naṉṟuṇ ṭākuṅkāl ataṉ payaṉākiya iṉpattai ūḻviṉaiyeṉa vuṇarntu nukaruvōr tītuṇṭākuṅ kāl atuvēyuṇarntu varuntātoḻital vēṇṭum eṉṟum, ūḻiṉum peruvali yuṭaiyatu piṟitu yātum illai yeṉṟum aṟivuṟuttiṉār.
ivvāṟu uraittārāyiṉum, ūḻviṉaiyē eṉṟu collikkoṇṭu ceyyakkaṭava aṟaṅkaḷaiyum, īṭṭaṟkuriya poruḷkaḷaiyum nukartaṟkuriya nukarcci kaḷaiyum oruvaṉ ceyyā toḻital kūṭātu eṉṟu tiruvaḷḷuvar vaṟpuṟutti yuḷḷār. maṟṟoṉṟu cūḻiṉum tāṉ muntuṟum peru vali
yuṭaiya tāyiṉum, iṟaivaṉpāl uṇmai yaṉpukoṇṭu avaṉ poruḷcēr pukaḻ purintārai iv vūḻviṉai vantu cērum valiyuṭaiyataṉṟu eṉṟaṟku “iruḷcēr iruviṉaiyum cērā” eṉṟum, malai tuḷaṅkiṉum kaṭal vetumpiṉum maṉantuḷaṅ kāta vuḷḷamum muyaṟciyumuṭaiyārkku ivvūḻ puṟaṅkāṭṭiyōṭum puṉmaiyuṭaiyateṉṟaṟku, “ūḻaiyum uppakkam kāṇpar ulaiviṉṟit tāḻā tuñaṟṟu pavar” eṉṟum yāppuṟa eṭutturaittār.
III. tiruvaḷḷuvaraip paṟṟiya kataikaḷ
pulavar purāṇam kūṟum katai
tiruvaḷḷuvar, oḷavaiyār mutaliya nallōr cilaruṭaṉ piṟantavar. ivaruṭaiya tantai pakavaṉ eṉṉum pārppaṉar. tāy ātiyeṉṉum peyariṉar. pakavaṉār piramaṉ “aṅkicattiṉar”; ātiyār kalaimakaḷ “aṅkicattiṉar”. ivviruvarukkum makkaḷ eḻuvar piṟantaṉar. avaruḷ āṇ makkaḷ mūvar; avar, vaḷḷuvaṉ, kapilaṉ, cēramāṉ eṉpōrāvar. peṇ makkaḷ nālvar; avar uppai, uṟuvai, oḷavai, vaḷḷi eṉappaṭuvar. im makkaḷ evvevviṭaṅkaḷiṟ piṟantārkaḷō, avvavviṭattilēyē avarkaḷai aṉpiṉṟi viṭṭēkuvatu peṟṟōrākiya pakavaṉum ātiyum mēṟkoṇṭirunta koḷkai.
peṟṟōrāl kaiviṭappeṟṟu aḻutukoṇṭirunta vaḷḷu vaṉākiya kuḻaviyai, oru vēḷāḷaṉ kaṇṭu ārvattōṭu eṭuttuc ceṉṟu, “ik kaviṉ cēyai in nāḷ eḷitiṉil emakku nalkal mukkaṇāṉ aruḷē” eṉṟu karutip pōṟṟi vaḷarttu vantāṉ. piḷḷai tirumaṇattukkuriya cevviyeytiyapiṉ, avarukku ‘vācuki’ eṉṉum ariya peṇṇoruttiyai maṇam ceytaṉar. ciṉṉāṭkaḷil vēḷāḷaṉum avaṉ maṉaiviyum civaṉaṭiyaic cērntaṉar. tiruvaḷḷuvar tam maṉaiviyōṭu kūṭi muṉivarum viyakku māṟu illaṟam purintuvantār. vācukiyum taṉ oḻukkat tāl kaṟpukkellaiyāyc cāṉṟōrāl karutappeṟum perum pēṟu peṟṟaṉar. itaṉaip pulavar purāṇa muṭaiyār,
“	mācaṟu tavattiṉ mikka vaciṭṭamā muṉikku vāytta
 	tēcavir vaṭamīṉ cīrtti ceppuvār ceppun tōṟum
 	vācanāṇ malarpput tēḷām vaḷḷuvaṉ maṉaitaṉ māṭci
 	pēcalum ulaki leṉṟum peruvaḻak kāyiṟ ṟammā!”
eṉṟu kūṟukiṉṟār.
ivvāṟu vaḷḷuvar kaṟpuc ciṟanta vācukiyārōṭu vāḻn
tirunta iṭam mayilāppūr. avvūruḷ avar ēṉaimakkaḷ vāḻum iṭattil vāḻāmal ūrukkup puṟattē oru kuccuvīṭa maittukkoṇṭu ataṉkaṇ necavut toḻil ceytuvantār. akkālattētāṉ avar tirukkuṟaḷ nūlaic ceytār. itaṉōṭu cōtiṭam mutaliya tūya nūlkaḷ cilavaṟṟait tiruvaḷḷuvar ceytāreṉap pulavar purāṇam kūṟukiṟatu.
an nāḷil maturaiyil irunta tamiḻc caṅkattup pulavarkaḷ tarukkumikutiyāl, caṅkattiṟku nūleḻuti varum ēṉaip pulavarkaḷai ikaḻntu, avartam nūlaiyum kuṟṟam kūṟi vañcaṉai ceytu vantaṉar. “eṉṉa pāṭṭevarcoṉṉālum itupaḻam pāṭal eṉṉap paṉṉalum eḻutik kāṭṭum paṭiṟum” piṟa tīcceyalum avarkaḷ mēṟkoṇṭiruntaṉarām. vaḷḷuvar ataṉaiyaṟiyātu tām eḻutiyaruḷiya tirukkuṟaḷai eṭuttuk
koṇṭu maturait tamiḻc caṅkattil ataṉai araṅkēṟṟak karutic ceṉṟār.
celluṅkāl vaḻiyil orunāḷ perumaḻai poḻintatu. tiruvaḷḷuvar vaḻiyarukē niṉṟa ālamara moṉṟiṉ aṭiyil otuṅkiniṉṟaṉar. aṅkē avarkku muṉṉē oruvaṉ vantiruntāṉ. avaṉ peyar iṭaikkāṭaṉ eṉpatu. av viṭaikkāṭaṉ, “putta kattup pēyā ciṟṟilaiyiṉ kīḻvā”, eṉṟu pēroli ceytu aḻaittāṉ. vaḷḷuvarum aṅkē ceṉṟār. ciṟitupōtil maḻaiyum niṉṟatu. piṉṉar, avaṉ vaḷḷuvaraip pārttu avar varalāṟṟaik kēṭṭaṟintu, “uṉ kaiyil uṟṟa nūliṟ kūṟum oṇ poruḷ vakai yātu?” eṉṟu viṉaviṉāṉ. avaṟku vaḷḷuvar, “muṟṟa yāvaiyum moḻintuḷēṉ,” eṉṟu moḻintār. iṭaik kāṭaṉ, āṭṭiṉaṅkaḷukku ilaitaḻai paṟittutavum kōlaik kuṟaḷā
ciriyarkkuk kāṭṭi “itaṟkum pāṭal pāṭiyuḷ ḷāyō?” eṉṟu kēṭṭāṉ. uṭaṉē tiruvaḷḷuvar, “uraṉ eṉṉum tōṭṭi yāṉ ōraintum kāppāṉ, varaṉeṉṉum vaippiṟkōrvittu,” eṉṉum pāṭṭiṉai, tam tirukkuṟaḷ nūlilirunta paṭittuk kāṭṭiṉār. atu kēṭṭu, iṭaik kāṭaṉ perumakiḻvu koṇṭu, tāṉum tiruvaḷḷu varuṭaṉ maturaikkuc cellum karuttuṭaiyēṉ eṉṟu colli, avarōṭē maturaikkuc ceṉṟāṉ. iruvarum ciṟitaḷavu ceṉṟatum,. vaḻiyil oḷavaiyār varakkaṇṭu avarpāl taṅkaḷ karuttai veḷiyiṭṭaṉar. avarum tāṉ maturaikkuc celvatākat terivittār. piṉpu mūvarum maturai mūtūraic ceṉṟu cērntaṉar.
maturaiyil caṅkattaic cārnta cāṉṟōr cilar immūvaraiyum nōkki, “nīvir enta ūr?” eṉṟu kēṭṭaṉar. ataṟkut tiruvaḷḷuvar,
“entavū reṉṟīr iruntavūr nīrkēḷīr,
 antavūrc ceyti yaṟiyīrō - antavūr,
 muppāḻum pāḻāy muṭiviloru cūṉiyamāy
 appāḻum pāḻāy aṟum.
eṉṟu viṭaiyiṟuttār. 
piṉpu avarkaḷ vaḷḷuvarai nōkki, “nīvir yār?” eṉṟu viṉava, avar, “yāṉ vaḷḷuvaṉ,” eṉṟār. eṉṟalum, avarkaḷ caṟṟum matippiṉṟi, “kulaikkuntaṉmai koḷḷutikollō?” eṉa ikaḻntaṉar. avvikaḻcciyaip poruḷākak koḷḷāta vaḷḷuvaṉār, “kulaikkavē vantēṉ,” eṉṟār.
piṉṉar avarkaḷ iṭaikkāṭaṉai nōkkiṉar. iṭaik kāṭaṉār avarkaḷaip pārttu, “āṟṟaṅkarai yil irunta māmarattil oru kākkaiyiruntu katta, ataṉai ōṭṭum karuttuṭaiya iṭaicciṟuvaṉ oruvaṉ ataṉai eyyak kōliṉṟik kaiyaṟṟu olitta kuṟipput tōṉṟa oru pāṭṭup pāṭumiṉ,” eṉṟār. avarkaḷ, kākkai karaiyum olik kuṟippum iṭaicciṟuvaṉ kaiyaṟṟolikkum icaik kuṟippum, kuṟikka vakai yaṟiyātu mayaṅkiṉar avar, avarkaḷ cerukkaṭaṅka,
“āṟṟaṅ karaiyi ṉarukirunta māmarattil
 kākkai yiruntu kaḵkaḵkeṉak - kākkaitaṉai
 eyyakkō lillāmal icicic ceṉṟāṉ
 vaiyakkō ṉārtam makaṉ”
eṉṟa pāṭṭaip pāṭik kāṭṭiṉār. ivvāṟē oḷavaiyārum pāṭṭukkaḷ pāṭic caṅkappulavariṉ tarukkai yaḻittaṉar.
iṉi, caṅkappulavarkaḷ tirukkuṟaḷiṉ cīrmai kāṇṭal vēṇṭit tāmirunta caṅkappalakaiyiṉ mēlē ataṉai vaittaṉar. appalakai avvoru nūlukkumaṭṭil iṭam tantu appulavar kaḷaik kuḷattil vīḻttiṟṟu. atukaṇṭu viyappuṟṟa caṅkap pulavarkaḷ tirukkuṟaḷai viyantu taṉit taṉiyē ovvoru pāṭṭup pāṭi vaḷḷuvarai vāḻttiṉar.
maturait tamiḻccaṅkattil tāmeḻutiya tirukkuṟaḷai araṅ kēṟṟic ciṟappeytiya tiruvaḷḷuvar, pāṇṭi nāṭṭavar ceyta ciṟappukkaḷai ēṟṟuk koṇṭu tirumayilai vantaṭaintu muṉ pōl maṉaiyaṟam purintuvantār. atu pōtu tiruvālaṅ kāṭṭil kūttap perumāṉāṉa iṟaivaṉ kāḷiyoṭu vātak
kūttu iyaṟṟi mēmpaṭṭāṉ. akkālai aṅkē kūṭiyirunta tēvarkaḷai nōkki avaṉ “emmil veṟṟi yeytiyavaryāvar?” eṉa viṉava, tām aṟiya māṭṭāmai kūṟivaruntiṉar. iṟaivaṉār, “nīvir tirumayilai ceṉṟu tiruvaḷḷuvaraik kaṇṭu teḷintu varuka,” eṉap paṇittār. avarkaḷum makkaḷurukkoṇṭu tiruvaḷḷuvar maṉaikkuc ceṉṟaṉar. aṅkē aṟputaṅkaḷ pala naṭantaṉa.
orunāḷ tiruvaḷḷuvar paḻaiyatu uṇṇuṅkāl, tam maṉaiviyai nōkki, “peṇṇē! itu cuṭukiṟatu, viciṟi koṇarka,” eṉṟār. vācukiyār maṟuttoṉṟum kūṟāmal viciṟi koṇarntu viciṟiṉār. appōtu paḻañcōṟṟil āvi kiḷampiṟṟu. vēṟoru nāḷvācukiyār taṇṇīr mukattaṟkuk kayiṟṟil kuṭamoṉṟaik kaṭṭik kiṇaṟṟil iṭṭirukkaiyil, tiruvaḷḷuvar avarai yaḻaittār. maṉaiviyār kayiṟṟai aṅṅaṉē viṭṭuviṭṭu vantār. kiṇaṟṟiṟ pātiyaḷavil vantirunta kuṭam avar tirumpi varumaḷavum “antaramāy” niṉṟu koṇṭiruntatu. ic ceyalkaḷaik kaṇṭa tēvarkaḷ, vācukiyāriṉ kaṟpunalam kaṇṭu viyantu pārāṭṭit tām vanta karuttait tiruvaḷḷuvarkkut terivittaṉar. avarkaṭku viṭai kūṟaluṟṟa tiruvaḷḷuvar,
“pūvi layaṉum purantaraṉum pūvulakait
 tāvi yaḷantōṉum tāmirukka- nāvil
 iḻainakki nūṉeruṭum ēḻai yaṟivēṉō
 kuḻainakkum piññakaṉṟaṉ kūttu”
eṉṟu moḻintār. tēvarkaḷ ataṉāl maṉantēṟit tam tēvavulaku ceṉṟu cērntaṉar.
ciṟitu kālattukkuppiṉ ēlēlaciṅkaṉ eṉṉum ceṭṭi oruvaṉ tiruvaḷḷuvaraik kuruveṉakkoṇṭu aruḷurai peṟṟu uyti peṟa eṇṇi vantāṉ. avaṉai vaḷḷuvar palavakaiyāl ārāyn tār. orunāḷ avar avaṉai oru marattil ēṟṟi iru kaiyaiyum viṭṭu niṟkumāṟu kūṟiṉār. avaṉ “kurupātam tuṇai” eṉa niṉaintu avvāṟē ceytāṉ. avaṉ kīḻē viḻavillai. ataṉpiṉ avaṉ tiruvaḷḷuvar maṉaiyil vācukiyārkku makaṉpōl oḻuki vantāṉ.
akkālattē nāṭṭil peruvaṟam uṇṭāyiṟṟu. āṉāl, ēlēlaciṅkaṉiṭam kuṉṟupōl celvam kuvin tiruntatu. avaṉaip pārttu, “nī vāṅkik kuvittuḷḷa neṟkuvaiyai vāṅkiyataṟku mēl orupaṭi mikutiyāka viṟpāyāka!” eṉṟār. avaṉum avvāṟē ceytāṉāka, neṟkuvai ciṟitum kuṉṟāmal kuvintē yiruntatu, appōtu, avar “itaṉai vaṟiyārkku varaiviṉṟi vaḻaṅkuka,” eṉṟār. avvaḻiyum atu kuṟai
paṭāmalē iruppa tāyiṟṟu. piṟitorukāl avar ēlēlaciṅ
kaṉai nōkki, “nī īṭṭiya poṉṉai urukkic caturakkal pōṟ ceytu ataṉmēl uṉ peyaraip poṟittuk kaṭalil eṟintu varuka,” eṉak kaṭṭaḷai yiṭṭār. avar colvaḻiyē avaṉum ceytu māṇavaṉ eṉṉum taṉatu uṇmaip pāṉmaiyai nilaināṭṭiṉāṉ.
itu niṟka, ēlēlaciṅkaṉ pulāluṇṇum iyalpiṉaṉ. ataṉai muṟṟavum kaiviṭuvateṉpatu avaṉukku aruñ
ceyalāka iruntatu. ataṉaiyaṟinta tiruvaḷḷuvar avaṉaip paṭippaṭiyākat tiruttak karuti avaṉai nāṭōṟum cettup
pōṉa cēl mīṉ kaḷaiyē tiṉṉu māṟu tiṭṭam ceytār. avvāṟē avaṉ ceytuvantāṉ. avaṉukkāka mīṉ valaiñar cetta cēl mīṉaik koṇarntu tantuvantaṉar. orunāḷ ammīṉ valaiñar periyatoru cēlmīṉai valaivīcip piṭittuk koṉṟu ataṉ uṭalaip piḷantārkaḷ. ataṉ vayiṟṟil pāci mūṭiya poṟkaṭṭi yoṉ ṟiruntatu ataṉai uyarntatoru paccaik kalleṉak karutiya mīṉvalaiñar  ēlēlaciṅkaṉiṭam tantaṉar. avaṉ ataṉaip poṉṉeṉa niṉaiyātu taṉ puṟak
kaṭaiyil iṭṭāṉ. nāḷaṭaivil atu pāci nīṅki muṉṉē avaṉ kaṭalil eṟinta caturap poṟ kallāka iruntatu. atu kaṇṭu viyanta ciṅkaṉ tiruvaḷḷuvarkkut terivittāṉ. avar, “aṟattāṟṟiṟ ceyta poruḷ akaṟṟiṉum akalātu,” eṉac colli avaṉait tēṟṟiṉār.
itu nikaḻnta cilapala nāṭkaḷukkuppiṉ, vācuki yārkku vāḻnāḷ ellai kuṟukiṟṟu. avar uyir eḷitil nīṅkātu ūcalāṭuvatāyiṟṟu. atukaṇṭa tiruvaḷḷuvar, “peṇṇē! uṉ maṉattil yātu uḷatu?” eṉṟār. appōtu, vācukiyār, “aṭikaḷē! aṭiyēṉ maṉattil ōr aiyam uḷatu. yāṉ uṇavu paṭaikkum pōtellām oru vaḷḷattil taṇṇīrum ōr ūciyum koṇarntu vaikkumāṟu paṇittirun tīrkaḷ. yāṉum atu ceytēṉāyiṉum tāṅkaḷ avaṟṟai orunāḷum kaiyāṇṭa
tillai. ataṟkuk kāraṇam aṟiya vēṇṭumeṉṟōr avā uṇṭu,” eṉṟār. atukēṭṭu uḷḷaṅkalaṅkiya tiruvaḷḷuvar, cōṟu paṭaikkumpōtu cintu māyiṉ, ataṉai ūciyāṟ kōttuk kiṇṇattil kaḻuviyuṇpataṟ kāka avaṟṟai vaikkumāṟu paṇittiruntēṉ; nī oru nāḷum cōṟu cintiyatillai,” eṉṟār. ic coṟ kaḷaik kēṭṭu maṉavamaiti peṟṟa vācukiyārkkuc ciṟitupōtil uyir nīṅkiṟṟu. avar pirivāṟṟāmal tiruvaḷḷuvar,
“	aṭiciṟ kiṉiyāḷai yaṉpuṭai yāḷaip
 	paṭicoṟ paḻināṇu vāḷai- aṭivaruṭip
 	piṉtūṅki muṉṉeḻunta pētaiyai yāṉpirintāl
 	eṉtūṅkum eṉkaṇ irā”
eṉṟu pāṭi varuntiṉar.
cilanāṭkaḷuppiṉ vaḷḷuvar, ēlēlaciṅkaṉai nōkki, “iṉṉum cilapōtil yāṉ ivvulakiṉiṉṟum nīṅkuvēṉ,” eṉṟu kūṟi, “yāṉ iṟantapiṉ eṉ irukālkaḷiṉum vaikkōṟ puriyaik kaṭṭi iḻuttuc ceṉṟu, ivvūrkku vaṭakkiluḷḷa kāṭṭil eṟintu viṭuka,” eṉṟu coṉṉār. avar kūṟiyavāṟē cila nāṭkaḷukkuppiṉ avar uyir uṭaliṉ nīṅkic ceṉṟatu. aṅkirun tōr kūṟiyavaṟṟāl maṉam pētuṟṟa ēlēlaciṅkaṉ, avar uṭalait tirumuḻukkāṭṭi oru tērmēl vaittu eṭuttēkat talaip paṭṭāṉ. uṭaṉē tiruvaḷḷuvar uyirt
teḻuntu, “avvāṟu ceyvatu kūṭātu; yāṉ muṉpu kūṟiyavāṟu ceyvatē vēṇṭuvatu,” eṉa ēlēlaciṅkaṉaip paṇittār. atu kēṭṭu naṭukkamuṟṟa ciṅkaṉ oruvāṟu tēṟi avar paṇitta
vaṇṇamē ceytāṉ. avar uṭalait tiṉṟa kākamum kaḻukum viṇṇavarāy vīṭupeṟṟaṉa. avar uṭal tīṇṭappeṟṟavarum uyarmikka tēvarāyiṉar.
ic ceytiyaik kaccippatiyil irunta ciṟpiyoruvaṉ aṟintu, avaraippōl tiruvuru oṉṟaic ceytu tirumayilaiyil ākamamuṟaippaṭi niṟuviṉāṉ. aḵtu iṉṟum yāvarum kaṇṭu paravip paṇiyumāṟu viḷaṅki varukiṟatu.
tiruvaḷḷuvamālai kuṟikkum katai
tiruvaḷḷuvar maturaic caṅkattil caṅkap palakaiyēṟit tamatu tirukkuṟaḷai araṅkēṟṟat toṭaṅkiṉār. appōtu, ap palakaiyil avarōṭu uṭaṉirukkattakkavar yār eṉṟa aiyam, pulavariṭaiyē yuṇṭāyiṟṟu. appōtu vāṉoli (acarīri) tōṉṟi, “teyvat tiruvaḷḷuvarōṭu caṅkap palakaik
kaṇ uruttiracaṉmar uṭaṉiruppārāka,” eṉṟatu. piṉpu, uruttira
caṉmar uṭaṉiruppa, vaḷḷuvar tam tirukkuṟaḷai araṅ
kēṟṟiṉār.
caṅkap pulavaruḷ cīttalaic cāttaṉār eṉa oruvar iruntār. avar tavaṟuṭaip pākkaḷaik kēṭka nērum pōtellām tam talaiyiṟ kuttikkoḷvatu vaḻakkam. tiruvaḷḷuvar tam tirukkuṟaḷai araṅkēṟṟiyapōtu tāṉ avar talaiyiṟ kuttikkoḷḷātu makiḻntu kēṭṭār.
ñāṉāmirta urai kūṟum katai
intiratattaṉ, vararuci, yāḷitattaṉ eṉṟa mūvar, tam viṉaittoṭakkai velvatu kuṟittu vēṟuvēṟu vakaiyiṟ ceṉṟaṉar. avaruḷ yāḷitattaṉ oru caṇṭāḷap peṇṇaik kāmuṟṟukkūṭip piṉ avaḷaik kiṇaṟṟilē taḷḷiviṭṭāṉ. avaḷai oru pārppaṉaṉ eṭuttucceṉṟu ātarittu vaṭanāṭṭiṟkuk koṇṭu pōṉāṉ. an nāṭṭiṟkuc ceṉṟa yāḷitattaṉ aṅkē appulaip peṇṇait taṉakku ēṟṟavaḷ eṉa maṇam ceytu koṇṭāṉ. avaḷ peyar āti yeṉpatu. avaṭku makkaḷ eḻuvar piṟantaṉar. avveḻu varuḷ tiruvaḷḷuvar oruvar.
kapilarakaval kūṟum katai
pakavaṉ eṉpavar ōr aruntavamuṉivar. āti yeṉpavaḷ karuvūr eṉṉum perumpatiyil vāḻnta oru pulaicci. ivviru varkkum piṟantavar kapilar.ivaroṭu āṇmakkaḷ mūvarum peṇmakkaḷ nālvarum piṟantaṉar. ivaruḷ uppaiyeṉpāḷ ūṟṟuk kāṭeṉṉum ūril vaṇṇār vīṭṭil vaḷarntāḷ.  uṟuvai yeṉpavaḷ maṟṟōr uṭaṉ piṟantāḷ. avaḷ kāvirip pūmpaṭṭiṉattil kaḷviṟpavar cēriyil cāṉṟār oruvar maṉaiyil vaḷarntāḷ. peṇmakkaḷuḷ oruvarāṉa oḷavaiyār pāṇcēri oṉṟil pāṇar vīṭṭil vaḷarntāḷ. vaḷḷi
yeṉpāḷ kuṟavar vāḻum kuṟicci yoṉṟil vāḻnta kuṟavar kōmāṉ perumaṉaiyil vaḷarntāḷ. toṇṭaimaṇṭalattut tirumayilaiyil paṟaiyar maṉaiyil vaḷḷuvar vaḷarntār. vañci nakarkkaṇ irunta atikamāṉ illattil atikamāṉ vaḷarntāṉ. tiruvārūril antaṇaṉ oruvaṉ maṉaiyil kapilar vaḷarntaṉar.
tirukkuṟaḷ ciṟappuppāyiraṅkaḷ kūṟum katai
pakavaṉuṭaiya tantai cōḻanāṭṭu antaṇarkaḷil oruvaṉ. pakavaṉ piṟantatum avaṉ nāṭkuṟippai ārāynta kaṇikaṉ, “pakavaṉ piṟkālattē puṉkulamātu oruttiyaik kūṭuvaṉ,” eṉṟaṉar. makaṉukku uṇṭāka irukkum tīviṉaikkuk kaḻuvāynāṭi, antaṇaṉ kācikkuppōṉāṉ. patiṉaintu āṇṭukaṭkup piṉ pakavaṉ, taṉ tāyāl taṉ tantai vaṭapulam ceṉṟirunta ceyti
kēṭṭut tāṉum piramacariyam pūṇṭu vaṭanāṭu nōkkic ceṉṟāṉ. ceṉṟavaṉ karuvūraiyaṭaintu ātiyeṉṉum pulaimakaḷiṉ peṇmai nalam kaṇṭu kātal mikkukkūṭip piṉ avaḷ pulaimakaḷ eṉpatu terintu avaḷaik kaiviṭṭēkiṉaṉ. āṉāl, ātiyeṉpāḷ avaṉai viṭātu piṉ toṭarn tāḷ. avaṉ uṭaṉē oru kallaiyeṭuttu avaḷ neṟṟiyil puṇṇuṇṭāmāṟu eṟintuviṭṭup pōyviṭṭāṉ. ātiyō, avaṉ varukaikuṟittu aṟaccālai oṉṟai niṟuvi naṭattivantāḷ.
vaṭapulattil taṉ tantaiyaip palaviṭaṅkaḷil tēṭiyum kāṇāta pakavaṉ, mīḷat taṉ cōḻanāṭṭukkut tirumpi vantāṉ. vantavaṉ vaḻiyil karuvūril ātiyiṉ aṟaccālaikku vantu cērntāṉ. avaṉai āti eṉpavaḷ aṟintu aṭi paṇintāḷ. avaṉ avaḷatu neṟṟivaṭuk kaṇṭu iṉṉāḷeṉa aṟintu muṉ pōl avaḷaik kaiviṭṭēkak karutiṉāṉ. avaḷ, “iṉi nī nīppiṉ yāṉ uyir vāḻēṉ,” eṉṟāḷ. avaṉ, “īṉṟiṭu makavelām īṉṟuḻi yēviṭut, tūṉṟiya tuṇipoṭu uṭaṉvara vallaiyēl, varuka,” eṉṟāṉ. avaḷum ataṟkicaintu avaṉuṭaṉ ceṉṟāḷ.
iruvarum celluṅkāl vaḻiyil uppai, oḷavai, uṟuvai, vaḷḷi yeṉṟa nāṉku peṇ makkaḷaiyum, atikamāṉ, vaḷḷuvar, kapilar eṉṟa mūṉṟu āṇ makkaḷaiyum peṟṟuviṭṭu nīṅkiṉar. am makavukaḷ aṅkaṅkē kaṇṭōrāl vaḷarkkap
paṭṭaṉa. vaḷḷuvar mayilāppūril vaḷḷuvar maṉaiyil vaḷarntār. piṉpu avar civa tīkkai peṟṟuc civaneṟi niṉṟu, pūvaiciyar marapiṟ piṟanta vācuki yārai maṇantu illaṟampurintu varukaiyil tirukkuṟaḷai iyaṟṟiṉār.
caiṉariṭaiyil vaḻaṅkum katai
karamuṇṭaṉ eṉṉum vaiciyaṉukkum cīmatiyeṉṉum avaṉ maṉaivikkum makaṉākap piṟantavar ēlāccāri. avarē tirukkuṟaḷai iyaṟṟiyavar. ivar vaṭamoḻiyil pirakiruta moḻiyil pañcāstikāya cāram mutalāka sālammiya pākuṭam īṟāka uḷḷa aimpattiraṇṭu caiṉa nūlkaḷum vaṭamoḻiyil tatvārtta cūttiram, patumananti paccīs eṉṟa iraṇṭu nūlkaḷumtamiḻil tirukkuṟaḷum iyaṟṟiṉār. ivaruṭaiya māṇavaruḷ tiruvuḷḷam nayiṉāreṉpavar oruvar. ivarkaḷ tirumayilaiyil vāḻntu vantaṉar. ēlāccāriyār tām iyaṟṟiya tirukkuṟaḷai maturaic caṅkattil araṅkēṟṟi accaṅkappulavar cerukkaṭaṅki varumāṟu tiruvuḷḷam nayiṉāriṭam tantuviṭuttār. avar ceṉṟu avvāṟē ceytu pōntār. piṉpu ēlāccāriyār vantavācikkaṇmai yiluḷḷa poṉṉūruk karuki
lirukkum nīlakiriyil muṉicaṅkattuṭaṉ iruppārāyiṉar. tiruvuḷḷam nayiṉār araṅkēṟṟiyataṉāl. tamiḻarkaḷ tiruvaḷḷuva nāyaṉār tirukkuṟaḷai iyaṟṟiṉār eṉak kūṟukiṉṟaṉa reṉpar.
kēḷvi vaḻiyāṟ kūṟappaṭum ceytikaḷ
1. paṇṭaināḷil pāṇṭināṭṭu maturaimā nakaril irunta caṅkappulavarkaḷ civaperumāṉai ikaḻntārāka, ap perumāṉ appulavarkaḷatu cerukkaṭaṅku māṟu piramaṉait tiruvaḷḷu varākavum, tirumālai iṭaikkāṭarākavum, kalai
makaḷē oḷavai yārakavum makkaḷākat tōṉṟumāṟu paṇittaruḷiṉār.  
2. pakavaṉār peruñcākaraṉ eṉpavarukkum tiruvārūrp pulaicci oruttikkum piṟantavar. āti yeṉpavaḷ tavamuṉi yeṉpavarukkum aruṇmaṅkai yeṉṉum pārppaṉap peṇṇukkum makaḷākap piṟantavar. piṉpu, avaḷ uṟaiyūrp perum paṟaiyaṉāl vaḷarkkappaṭṭu mēlūr akarattil nītiyaiyar maṉaiyil vaḷarntāḷ.
3. pakavaṉ ātiyai mutaṟkaṇ tāṉ kācikkup pōm
pōtu mēlūr akarattil kaṇṭu avaḷatu iḷamaiyai eḷḷi aṭittut turattiṉāṉ. piṉpu appakavaṉ kāciyiliruntu tirumpi varuṅkāl avaḷatu peṇmai nalam kaṇṭu kāmuṟṟu maṇañceytu koṇṭāṉ.
4. tiruvaḷḷuvar kuḻavipparuvattil tirumayilaiyil ōr iluppai marattaṭiyil peṟṟōrāl kaiviṭap peṟṟār. piḷḷaipēṟu kuṟittut tavam kiṭanta vēḷāḷaṉ maṉaivi yorutti avaraik kaṇṭu eṭuttucceṉṟu, tāṉ peṟṟa piḷḷaipōl vaḷarttu vantāḷ.
5. “marapaṟiyāk kuḻantaiyai vaḷarkkiṟārkaḷ,” eṉṟu ūrār paḻikūṟa, tiruvaḷḷuvarai vaḷartta tāy tantaiyar avarai oru māṭṭut toḻuvil toṭṭiliṭṭukkiṭatti vaḷarttu
vantaṉar. aṟivu vantapiṉ tiruvaḷḷuvar oru paṉaimarattaṭiyil iruntu tavam purintaṉar. piṉpu tirumūlar mutaliya cāṉṟō
rōṭu kūṭit tavam ceyvārāyiṉar.
6. kāvirippākkattil mārkkacakāyaṉ eṉṉum vēḷāḷa ṉoruvaṉ āyiram ēr vaittup payir ceyyum peruñ celva
muṟṟu viḷaṅkiṉāṉ. avaṉukku vētāḷamoṉṟāl aṭikkaṭi iṭukkaṇ tōṉṟiya vaṇṇam iruntatu. ataṉāl avaṉ tiruvaḷḷuvarai yaṭaintu kuṟaiyirantu niṟka, avar avaṉatu kāvirip pākkattukku vantu vētāḷattiṉ āṟṟalaik keṭuttut turattiṉār. atukaṇṭu viyanta mārkkacakāyaṉ taṉ makaḷ vācuki yeṉpāḷait tiruvaḷḷu varukku maṇam ceytu koṭuttāṉ.
vācukiyait tirumaṇam ceytukoḷḷumuṉ tiruvaḷḷuvar vācukiyiṭam maṇalaittantu cōṟākac camaikkumāṟu paṇittār. avarum avvāṟē camaittut tantār. ataṉāl tiruvaḷḷuvar vācukiyai maṇañ ceytu koṇṭār.
7. tiruvaḷḷuvar tirumayilaiyil ēlēlaciṅkaṉ eṉṉum vaṇikaṉoruvaṉiṭam nūl peṟṟu neytaṟṟoḻil purintu vantār. annāḷil, tēvarkaḷ, civaṉatu ālaṅkāṭṭu āṭal nalam aṟiyavantu aṟivupeṟṟuc ceṉṟaṉar. ēlēlaciṅkaṉ orunāḷ civapūcai ceyyuṅkāl tamatu karuttaik kuppattu vāṇikattil celutti yiruntatait tiruvaḷḷuvar kūṟakkēṭṭu avaṉ tiruvaḷḷuvarkku māṇavarāyiṉaṉ.
8. orukāl ēlēlaciṅkar tamakku makkaṭpēṟu vēṇṭu meṉat tiruvaḷḷuvariṭam terivittu varuntiṉar. tiruvaḷḷuvar “āṇṭavaṉ aruḷāl aḵtu uṇṭām,” eṉa moḻintaṉar. ciṉṉāṭkaḷil āṇṭavaṉ oru kuḻaviyuruvāy vantu ēlēlaciṅkar muṉ kiṭantaṉar. ēlēlaciṅkarum ataṉait tiruvaḷḷuvarkkuk kāṭṭi ‘aḻakāṉantaṉ’ eṉap peyariṭṭu vaḷarttu vantār.
9. orukāl ēlēlaciṅkaruṭaiya kappal karaitaṭṭi viṭavē, tiruvaḷḷuvar “ēlaiyā” eṉṟu colli ataṉai iḻukkumāṟu collit tāmum uṭaṉiruntu iḻuttār. kappal iṉitu karai yaṭaintatu.
10. mīṉ valaiñar koṇarnta poṟ kaṭṭiyaik kalleṉṟu karutiya ēlēlaciṅkar tamatu maṉaiyiṉ puṟakkaṭaiyil iṭac ceytiruntār. atu pāci nīṅkiyatum poṟkaṭṭiyākat tōṉṟak kaṇṭu vaḷḷuvarkkut teri vittu uṇmai yuṇarntār.
11. annāḷil vāḻnta pulavarkaḷ ceykaiyāl neṭuṅ kālamāy maṉattuyaraṭaintirunta aḻakāṉantar mutaliyōr vēṇṭukōḷukku icaintu tiruvaḷḷuvar tirukkuṟaḷaip pāṭi yaruḷiṉar.
12. maturaiyil cerukkaṭaṅkiya pulavarkaḷum, ukkirap peruvaḻuti yeṉṉum pāṇṭiyaṉum tiruvaḷḷuvarai vaṇaṅkip potikai malaikkuc ceṉṟu akattiyarkkut tirukkuṟaḷaik kāṭṭumāṟu kēṭṭuk koḷḷavē, tiruvaḷḷuvar tirukkuṟaḷai avarkkuk kāṭṭi avarai makiḻvittaṉar.
13. orukāl koṅkaṇareṉṉum cittaroruvar vantu koṇṭirukkaiyil, vāṉattiṟ paṟantu ceṉṟa kok koṉṟu avarmēl eccamiṭṭatu. ataṉai avar vekuṇṭu nōkkiṉārāka, akkokku uṭaṉē erintu cāmparāyiṟṟu. piṉṉar accittar vācukiyirunta maṉaippuṟattē niṉṟu uṇavu kēṭṭār. appōtu vācukiyār tiruvaḷḷuvarkku uṇavaḷittukkoṇṭiruntamaiyāl, puṟattē vantu uṇavaḷittaṟkuc ciṟitu kālamāyiṟṟu. koṅkaṇar ciṉamuṟṟu. vācuki yārai uṟuttu nōkkiṉar. vācukiyār, avar tiṭukkiṭumāṟu, “kokkeṉṟu niṉaittaṉaiyā, koṅkaṇavā?” eṉṟār. koṅkaṇar naṭunaṭuṅki avaratu kaṟpumāṇpu kaṇṭu vaṇaṅkiṉar.
14. koṅkaṇacittar tiruvaḷḷuvarpāl ñāṉam peṟṟu, illaṟam, tuṟavaṟam eṉṟa iraṇṭaṉuḷ ciṟantatu kāṇa viḻain taṉar. tiruvaḷḷuvar avarait tamatu maṉaiyiṉkaṇ cilanāḷ taṅkiyirukkumāṟu paṇittār. vācukiyiṉ maṉai māṭcikaṇṭu viyantakoṅkaṇar, “kaṟpuṭaiya maṉaivi vāykkiṉ, tuṟavaṟat tiṉum illaṟamē ciṟanta,” teṉat tēṟic ceṉṟaṉar.
15. orunāḷ tiruvaḷḷuvar neytaṟṟoḻilaic ceytu koṇṭirukkaiyil avar kaiyilirunta kuḻal kainnaḻuvik kīḻē vīḻntuviṭṭatu. ataṉai eṭuppataṟku viḷakkēṟṟik koṇarumāṟu tiruvaḷḷuvar vācukiyāraip paṇittār. avarum avvāṟē ceytār.
16. tiruvaḷḷuvar mācit tiṅkaḷil uttaranāḷil maṇṇu lakai nīttaṉar.
tiruvaḷḷuvaratu 
varalāṟṟu ārāycci
1. peṟṟōr 
pulavar purāṇamum kēḷvivaḻic ceytiyum, ciṟappup pāyiraṅkaḷum orumukamākat tiruvaḷḷuvaruṭaiya tantai peyar pakavaṉ eṉṟum, tāy peyar āti yeṉṟum kūṟukiṉṟaṉa. ñāṉā mirtameṉṉum nūluraimaṭṭil tiruvaḷḷuvaruṭaiya tantai peyar yāḷitattaṉ eṉṟum, tāy ātippulaicci yeṉṟum kūṟukiṉṟatu. pulavar purāṇa mutaliyavaṟṟai nōkka, ñāṉāmirta urai paḻamaiyāṉatu: āyiṉum, atu kūṟum ceyti nāṭṭil payila vaḻaṅkavillai. mēlum, yāḷitattaṉ katai, ēṉai vararuci, intira tattaṉ mutaliyōr kataikaḷōṭu viraviyē ñāṉā
mirtattuṭ kāṇappaṭukiṟatu. ik kataikaḷ; kuṇāṭṭiyar eṉpavarāl paicāca moḻiyil eḻutappeṟṟa pirukaṟ kataiyeṉappaṭum peruṅkataiyil aṭaṅkiya puṉainturaikkataikaḷām eṉpatu ataṉ vaṭanūl moḻipeyarppāl viḷakkamuṟukiṉṟatu. aṉṟiyum, ñāṉāmirtam kūṟum, “yāḷi. kūvaṟṟūṇṭum ātip
pulaicci kātaṟkācaṉi yāki mētiṉi, iṉṉicai yeḻuvarp payantōḷ” eṉṟa aṭikaḷum yāḷitattaṉ ātip pulaicciyaik kūṭit tiruvaḷḷuvaraip payantāṉ eṉṟu kūṟukiṉṟila. ataṉ paḻaiyavuraiyoṭu kūṭiya ēṭukaḷ “eḻuvareṉṟatu cunantar mutaliya eḻuvarai” yeṉṟu kuṟikkiṉṟaṉa. ñāṉāmirta accuppiratiyil kāṇappaṭum uraitāṉ “eḻuvar kapilaraiyuḷḷiṭṭa eḻuvar” eṉṟu uraikkiṟatu. itaṟkuriya urai vēṟupāṭu cunantar mutaliya eḻuvareṉpatu, inta ñāṉāmirtat tukkum tiruvaḷḷu varukkum yātōr iyaipumillai yeṉṟaṟkup pōtiya cāṉṟākiṟatu.
iṉi, tiruvaḷḷuvaruṭaiya peṟṟōr pakavaṉum ātiyumāvar eṉak kūṟum piṟaceytikaṭku ātaravu oṉṟum itukāṟum kiṭaittilatu. tirukkuṟaḷil mutaṟkuṟaḷ, ulakiṟku mutal itu veṉṟaṟku “āti pakavaṉ” eṉṟu kūṟuvatu koṇṭu, ātiyeṉpatu tāyatu peyareṉṟum, pakavaṉeṉpatu tantaiyatu peyareṉṟum koḷḷap paṭukiṉṟaṉa eṉpar cilar ivvāṟu tammaip peṟṟa tāytantaiyar peyarkaḷaik kuṟippākavō veḷippaṭaiyākavō kuṟittōtiya tamiḻ nūlāciriyar oruvarum ilar. eṉavē, avvāṟu ōtuvatu tamiḻc cāṉṟōr marapaṉṟu eṉpatu teḷivākiṟatu. ataṉāl, tiruvaḷḷuvaraip peṟṟa tāy āti yeṉavum, tantai pakavaṉ eṉavum koḷvatu ārāycci yaṟiñar koḷḷat takuvataṉṟām.
iṉi, kapilarakavalum, pulavar purāṇamum, piṟavum oru mukamākak kūṟutalkoṇṭu, tiruvaḷḷuvaruṭaiya peṟṟōr pakava ṉārum ātiyārumāvar eṉṟu koḷvatāyiṉ, aḵtu ārāytaṟ kuriyatēyām. tiruvaḷḷuvar kālattē āṭavar ātaṉ eṉṟu peyarpeṟṟu vāḻntatupōla makaḷir ātiyeṉṉum peyaruṭaṉ, “ātimantiyār” eṉṟāṟpōla iruntatuṇṭēyaṉṟi, pakavaṉ eṉṟa peyar tāṅkiyirunta tillai. mukkōlēntit tirinta muṉivaraip pakavar eṉa vaḻaṅkiya tuṇṭu. itaṉāl, tiruvaḷḷu varuṭaiya tāy peyar ātiyār eṉal poruntumeṉpatum, tantai peyar pakavaṉeṉal iyaṟpeyarāka amaiyāteṉpatum viḷaṅkukiṉṟaṉa.
iṉi, ātiyāraip pulaicciyeṉavum, pakavaṉaip pārppaṉa antaṇareṉavum tiruvaḷḷuvar varalāṟu paṟṟiya kuṟippukkaḷ kūṟak kāṇkiṉṟōm. mukkōlēntit tirinta tuṟavikaḷākiya antaṇar kaḷaip pakavareṉpatu paṇṭai nāḷai vaḻakku. ataṉāl, pakavaṉai antaṇareṉṟal oru vāṟu amaiyum. aracar, vaṇikar, vēḷāḷar, pārppaṉar eṉpāruḷ yāvarum tuṟavikaḷākiyapōtu antaṇa reṉappaṭuvar. “antaṇareṉpōr aṟavōr” eṉat tiruvaḷḷuvarē kuṟippatu kāṇka. ākavē, pakavaṉākiya antaṉar pārppaṉarākat tāṉ irukkavēṇṭumeṉa varaintu kūṟal kuṟṟamām. kapilar mutaliya cāṉṟōrākiya pārppaṉar antaṇarātaliṉ. avaraip payantavar pakavaṉ eṉappaṭutaliṉ, pakavaṉai antaṇaṉ eṉap piṟkālattavar karutiyirukkalām. kapilar antaṇareṉap paṭaṟ kēṟpa maṇamum makappēṟu miṉṟit tuṟavu vāḻvu vāḻntavar. pakavaṉākiya pārppaṉarum antaṇarāyiṉ, avar ātiyai maṇantā reṉṟum, tiruvaḷḷuvar mutaliyōraip peṟṟā reṉṟum, aruḷ eṉpatu ciṟitumiṉṟit tām peṟṟa makkaḷai āṅkāṅkuk kaiviṭṭēkiṉareṉṟum kūṟappaṭuvaṉa aṉaittum poyyuraikaḷām.
iṉi, ātiyāraip pulaicciyeṉpar. tiruttaku teyvat tiruvaḷḷuvaraip peṟṟa peṟalarun tirumikka avvammaiyārai, “maṅkaiyarkkut taṉiyaraci eṅkaḷ teyvam,” eṉa maṉamārap pōṟṟi vaḻipaṭavēṇṭiya kaṭamai tamiḻc cāṉṟōrkku uṇṭākiṟatu. avvāṟu ceyyātu, “pulaicci”yeṉa iḻittup pēcuvatē vaḻakkātaliṉ, avarukkum tiruvaḷḷuvarkkum toṭarpillai yeṉpatu teḷivākiṟatu. nalattiṉkaṇ nāriṉmai
koḷvatu kulattukku mācu ceyvateṉpatait teḷiya
vuṇarntavar  tamiḻc cāṉṟōr.
iṉi, pulāluṇpōr aṉaivarum muṟkālattē nūlōrāl pulaiyareṉappaṭṭaṉar. pulāluṇṇum oruvaṉaip pulaiyaṉ eṉavum, pulāluṇṇum oruttiyaip pulaicciyeṉavum, vaḻaṅkuvar. “pollāp pulālai nukarum pulaiyarai” (tiruman. 199) eṉat tirumūlar kūṟuvatu kāṇka. paṇṭaināḷil pārppār mutaliya palarum pulāl uṇṭaṉa reṉpatu ārāycciyāḷarkkut terinta ceytiyākum. aṅṅaṉamāka, ātiyāraimaṭṭil pulaicciyeṉṟu kūṟuvatu poyyām. ivvāṟṟāl, ātiyākiya pulaicciyait tiruvaḷḷuvar varalāṟṟil iṇaitturaippatu peruṅ kuṟṟamām.
ivvāṟē, tiruvaḷḷuvaraip peṟṟa tāytantaiyar tām peṟṟa makkaḷai āṅkāṅkē aṉpiṉṟik kaiviṭṭuc ceṉṟaṉareṉa oru kuṟippum kūṟappaṭukiṟatu. avvaṇṇam ceyyakkūṭiya vaṉkaṇmai ettakaiya tāyarpālum eḷitiṟ kāṇakkūṭiyataṉṟu; pakavaṉārum ātiyārum koṇṭa poruntā maṇattaip puṉaintōr, tīneṟiyil makap peṟṟōr ceyyum icceyalai ivarkaṇ mēlēṟṟip paṭaittu moḻintuḷḷaṉar eṉak koṇṭoḻivatu tavira. itaṉai uṇmaiyeṉak koḷḷutaṟku iṭam illai.
2. uṭaṉpiṟantār
iṉi, tiruvaḷḷuvaraiyuḷḷiṭṭa eḻuvar ittāy tantai yarkkup piṟanta makkaḷ eṉpatu tiruvaḷḷuvar varalāṟṟiṟ kāṇappaṭum kuṟippākum. avveḻuvaraiyum nōkkuṅkāl, ikkuṟippaip paṭaittu moḻintōr ilakkiya aṟivō kālavuṇarvō ciṟitumillāta veḷiṟṟaṟiviṉareṉpatu teḷivākiṟatu. oḷavai, vaḷḷi, atikamāṉ, kapilar eṉpōr caṅka ilakkiyaṅ kaḷiṟ kāṇappaṭum cāṉṟōrāvar. ivarkaḷatu varalāṟum ōraḷavu kaṟṟōraṉaivarkkum iṉitu terintirukkiṟatu. atu kāṇpōr, ivarkaḷai oru tāy vayiṟṟiṟ piṟantavarkaḷ eṉa orukālum uṭaṉpaṭār.
mēlum, tiruvaḷḷuvaruṭaiya matinuṭpattaiyum, ulakiyalaṟi vaiyum nōkkiṉ, ivar oru celvakkuṭiyil piṟantu vaḷarntu centamiḻvaḷam ciṟakkappeṟṟavar eṉpatu teḷivāka viḷaṅkukiṟatu. ellāc cāṉṟōrkaḷum ivarait tiruvaḷḷuvareṉṟē vaḻaṅki yuḷḷaṉar ilakkaṇa vuraikārarkaḷ, ‘vaḷḷuvaṉ’ eṉpatu ivaratu iyaṟ peyareṉṟē karutukiṉṟaṉar. ākavē, vaḷḷuvareṉpata ivarkkut tāytantaiyariṭṭa iyaṟpeyareṉṟum, ivaruṭaiya oppuyarvaṟṟa pulamai nalaṅ
kaṇṭu viyappuk koṇṭa cāṉṟōr, tiruvaḷḷuvareṉavum ‘teyvappulavaṉ tiruvaḷḷuvaṉ’ eṉavum pārāṭṭic ciṟap pippārāyiṉa reṉṟu aṟital vēṇṭum.
3. ūr
iṉi, tiruvaḷḷuvaṉāratu ūr tirumayilai (mayilāppūr) eṉpatu perumpāṉmaiyāṉa vaḻakku. caiṉa camayattavar iṭaiyil vaḻaṅkum kataiyum tirumayilaiyaik kuṟikkiṟatu; tiruvaḷḷuva mālaiyoṉṟumē ivaratu iṭam maturaiyeṉak kuṟikkiṉṟatu. itaṟkēṟpavē, tiruvaḷḷuvar nūṟku uraikaṇṭa parimēlaḻakar toṇṭaināṭṭiṉareṉak kaṇṭa toṇṭai maṇṭala catakam, “tirukkāñcivāḻ parimēlaḻakaṉ vaḷḷuvaṉārkku vaḻikāṭṭiṉāṉ toṇṭai maṇṭalamē” (41) eṉṟu kūṟi, vaḷḷuvaṉārkkum iṭam ittoṇṭaināṭē eṉa eṭuttōtu
kiṉṟilatu. tirumayilai toṇṭaināṭṭiṉ kaṇṇatōrūr eṉpatai,
“kayilaiyuṅ* kacciyum viṟkōla mumnaṟ kaḻukkuṉṟamum
 payilvaḻi tāyamum pācūru macciṟu pākkamunal
 layilaiyum vallan tiruvoṟṟi yūru maraciliyum
 mayilaiyum īcaṉ makiḻntataṉṟō toṇṭai maṇṭalamē.”
eṉṟu kuṟittōtukiṟatu. tirumayilaiyil uḷḷa tiruvaḷḷuvar kōyilum piṟkālattē kaṭṭappeṟṟa teṉpatai muṉpē kūṟiṉōm. ivvāṟṟāl, tiruvaḷḷuvarkku ūr tirumayilai eṉakkūṟuvatu oru talaiyākat tuṇitaṟ killāmaiyum, eṉavē, tiruvaḷḷuva mālai kūṟuvatē valiyuṟuvatum kāṇappaṭukiṉṟaṉa.
4. maṉai vāḻvu
iṉi, tiruvaḷḷuvar vācuki yeṉpavarai maṇantu illaṟam purintuvantaṉareṉṟa ceyti, mēṟkūṟiya kēḷvivaḻi varalāṟṟilum ēṉaip purāṇa mutaliya vaṟṟilum oppak kūṟappaṭukiṟatu ātiyār pakavaṉār eṉṉum peyarkaḷai nōkka. vācuki yeṉṉum peyar ārāyccikkuriya toṉṟākiṟatu. vācuki yeṉṉum vaṭamoḻip peyar, annāḷil tamiḻnāṭṭil vaḻaṅkiya tamiḻar peyarākat tōṉṟavillai. vaṭavarum, avaratu vaṭamoḻiyum viraviyirunta kālattē tiruvaḷḷuvar vāḻntārāyiṉum vaṭamoḻip peyaruḷ vācuki yeṉpatu pōlum peyar makkaṭku vaḻaṅkiyatillai. tammai vaṭavarākak karuti yoḻukum innāḷaip pārppaṉarum tām tamakku vaṭamoḻip peyaraiyiṭṭu vaḻaṅkiya vaḻakkam. nāyakka maṉṉar āṭciyiṟṟāṉ perukiṟṟeṉpataip pallāyirak kaṇakkāka eṭukkappaṭṭirukkum teṉṉāṭṭuk kal veṭṭukkaḷ eṭuttōtukiṉṟaṉa pārppaṉarum ki.pi. patiṉaintu patiṉāṟām nūṟṟāṇṭuvaraiyil vaṭamoḻip peyaraip peritum tamakku iṭṭu vaḻaṅkātiruntaṉarāka. pārppaṉarallātārākiya tiruvaḷḷu varum piṟarum tam makaḷirkku vaṭamoḻip peyariṭṭu vaḻaṅkiṉareṉak kūṟuvatu uṇmaiyuraiyākātu. ākavē, tiruvaḷḷuvarkku maṉaivi yār oruvar iruntaṉareṉpatu uṇmaiyāyiṉum, avar peyar vācuki yeṉa vaḻaṅkiṟṟeṉpatu porutta maṉṟeṉṟu kāṇ kiṉṟōm ivvāṟē, vācukiyār tantai peyar mārkkacakāya mutaliyāreṉpatum muḻutta poyyuraiyākiṟatu. kāvirippākkat tukku mēṟkil pālāṟṟiṉ karaiyil irukkum viriñci purattuk kōyililuḷḷa iṟaivaṉai aṅkuḷḷa paḻaṅ kalveṭṭukkaḷ vaḻittuṇai nāyaṉā reṉṟum, piṟkālak kalveṭṭukkaḷ mārkkacakāyīccura reṉṟum kuṟikkiṉṟaṉa. mārkkacakāyaṉeṉṟa iṟaivaṉ peyar nāṭṭil vaḻaṅkiya piṟkālattē makkaḷ tamakkum mārkkacakāyaṉeṉap peyar koṇṭiruntaṉarātalāl, ap peyaruṭaiyār oruvar, tiruvaḷḷuvar kālattē iruntā reṉpatu poruntātu. kāvirippākkattiṉ paḻam peyar kāvitippākkam eṉpataiyum īṇṭu niṉaivukūrtal vēṇṭum.  itaṉāl mārkka cakāyar varalāṟum piṟavum avar peyarpōlap piṟkālat teḻunta puṉainturaiyeṉak koḷvatē poruttam.
tiruvaḷḷuvar kaṟpuṭaiya maṉaiviyāruṭaṉ kūṭi vāḻntu varukaiyil necavuttoḻil purintuvantāreṉṟu kūṟi, “iḻai
nakki nūl neruṭum ēḻaiyaṟivēṉō,” eṉa avar tammaik kūṟuvatākac collum veṇpāvai ataṟkuc cāṉṟākak kāṭṭuvatu uṇṭu. āṉāl, attoḻil paṟṟiya kuṟippoṉṟum avaratu nūliṟ kāṇappaṭavillai. uḻavu, vāṇipam, araciyal, amaicciyal, tūtupōtal, pōr ceytal, maṟaiyōtutal mutaliya toḻilkaḷai āṅkāṅkuk kūṟuvatupōlavēṉum tamatu neytaṟṟoḻilai avar kuṟittilar. ātalāl, avar neytaṟṟoḻilaittāṉ tamatu toḻilākak koṇṭi runtāreṉak kūṟutaṟku iṭamillai.
tiruvaḷḷuvar kaṟpuṟu poṟpumikka maṉaivi yāroṭu kūṭi  illiruntu nallaṟam purintu varukaiyil, tamiḻ nāṭum tamiḻ makkaḷum uytal vēṇṭi, tām tam nallaṟivukoṇṭu ārāynta naṉṉūṟ poruḷ aṉaittaiyum naṉku teḷintu ulaka vāḻvil avvappōtu kaṇṭa ariya kāṭcikaḷaiyum tērntu, yāvarkkum oppap payaṉpaṭattakka nūliyaṟṟa vēṇṭumeṉṟa karuttāl untappeṟṟut teyvat tirukkuṟaḷ nūlaic ceytārātal vēṇṭum.
5. nūliyaṟṟal
ivar kālattē makkaḷ kūṭṭattilkaṭavuḷ uṇmaiyum, viṉaittoṭarpum, iṟaivaṉ tiruvaṭi yaṭaivatē vīṭupē
ṟeṉṉum vīṭupēṟṟuṇarvum tuṟakka narakaṅkaḷ uṇṭeṉṉum koḷkaiyum piṟavum nilaviṉa. vētiyar maṟaiyum tuṟaviyar tuṟavu neṟiyum viraviyiruntamaiyiṉ, ivarkālam, vaṭavaratu vētaneṟiyum tamiḻaratu caivaneṟiyum kalanta tiruneṟi oru
maruṅkum, putta camaṇarkaḷiṉ putuc camaya neṟi orumaruṅkum nilaviya kālamākum. iraṇṭaṉpayiṟciyāl makkaḷ kūṭṭam ulakiyalil cīriya nāṭṭam peṟātu cīrkulaivatāyiṟṟu. vaitika
neṟiyiṉar, piṟappil uyarvu tāḻvum, ulakiyalil nilaiyāmaik kuṟippum, ulaka vāḻvil veṟuppum piṟavum kaṟpittu, makkaḷai ulakiyaṟporuḷai ārāytaṟkum, makkaḷiṭaiyē orumaiyuṇarvu tōṟṟuvittu oṟṟumai yoṭu vāḻtaṟkum ūkkaṅkoḷḷātu vēṟuvēṟākap pirintu keṭutaṟku ulaivait toḻukiṉar. putta camaṇarkaḷ, pollā nōṉpum vāḻkkaiyiṟ paṟṟiṉmaiyum peruṅ koḷkai kaḷāka eṭuttōti, araciyal, camutāyam, poruḷīṭṭal mutaliya tuṟaikaḷil makkaḷ karuttu īṭupaṭāta nilaiyiṉaiyuṇṭu paṇṇiṉar. itaṉviḷaivē kaḷappirarum pallavarum piṟarum nāṭṭil nuḻaintu araciyalaik kaippaṟṟit tamiḻaratu toṉmai varavu keṭuttaṟku iṭam ceytatu eṉiṉ atu tavaṟākātu.
paṟṟukkoṇṭu ceyyum ilvāḻvu tuṉpattukku ētu; ‘puttēḷulakam putalvarum tārār,” eṉpatu puttarkaḷiṉ poru ḷuraiyākum.
ittakaiya koḷkaikaḷaikkēṭṭa teṉṉāṭṭavar, ivaṟṟai mēṟkoṇṭu orumai nōkkam peṟātu talaitaṭu māṟalāyiṉar. putumaikku aṭimaiyākum puṉmai tamiḻ makkaḷ
pāl iruppatukaṇṭu maṉam varuntiya tiruvaḷḷuvar, ulakiyaṟkup poruḷāvaṉa aṟamum poruḷum iṉpamum eṉap paṇṭaiyōr uraitta uṟutip poruḷkaḷaiyē mīḷavum eṭuttut tokuttum vakuttum virittum uraikka vēṇṭiya kaṭamai aṟivuṭaiya cāṉṟōrkku uḷatāvatai naṉku uṇarntār. ulakiyalil ceyyum nalvāḻvē vīṭupēṟṟukku vāyilām eṉpatai vaṟpuṟutta viḻaintār; vaiyattuḷ vāḻvāṅku vāḻvatu vāṉuṟaiyum teyva vāḻvām eṉpatu paḻantamiḻaratu vāḻkkaik kuṟikkōḷ. itaṉaiyē pallāṟṟāṉum ettiṟattōrum viḻaintēṟkum vaṇṇam nūl ceyvārāyiṉar. ivvuṇmai
yiṉai naṉku ārāyntu kaṇṭē kallāṭaṉār eṉpār, “camayak kaṇakkar mativaḻi kūṟātu, ulakiyal kūṟip poruḷitu veṉṟa vaḷḷuvaṉ,” eṉṟu viḷakkiṉār. ēṉaiyōrellām ivvulakiyalaik karuvi yākakkoṇṭu tattam camayak karuttiṉaiyē vaṟpuṟutta muyaṉṟaṉar.
ulakiyalai, aṟam, poruḷ, iṉpam eṉṟa mūṉṟu kuṟik kōḷiṉkaṇ vaittu ārāynta mutaṉmai tolkāppiyark kurittu. ataṉaip payaṉpaṭavuṇarntu uṇmaiyuṇarvāl makkaṭku vakuttum virittum uraitta uyarnilaipperumai tiruvaḷḷuvark kēyuriyatu. ivarpōl ulakiyaṟporuḷai ivvaṇṇam vakuttum virittum uraitta aṟiñar, ipparanta   maṇṇulakil evarumē ilar eṉpatai yāvarum oppavuraik
kiṉṟaṉar.
6. tirukkuṟaḷ taṉittamiḻ mutaṉūl
iṉi, ulakil kāṇappaṭum nūlkaḷuḷ mikavum paḻamai
yāṉatu vētam eṉpar. “ulakiyal vētanūl oḻukkam” eṉṟaṉar cēkkiḻār. atuvum ulakiyalaik kaṉmamum ñāṉamum eṉa iraṇṭāka vakuttōtuvatākak kūṟuvar āṉāl, ulakiyalil uyirkaḷ ceytaṟkuriyatu aṟam: ataṉāl ceyyappaṭuvatu poruḷ; ataṉāl avvuyirkaṭ kuḷatākum payaṉ iṉpam eṉṟu vakuttōtum aṟivuṭai yiyalpu vētattukkum kiṭaiyātu eṉpar.
ittirukkuṟaḷ nūl amaintuḷḷa muṟai vēṟē em moḻiyiṉum kāṇappaṭavillai; ākavē, itaṉ amaipput tiruvaḷḷuvar tāmē paṭaittukkoṇṭa toru taṉitta uṇmai muṟaiyākum. nilavulakil aṟivuṭai makkaḷ kāṇum uyirk koḷkai palavum itaṉkaṭ kāṇappaṭum;āṉāl, ittirukkuṟaḷiṉ kaṇṇuḷḷa koḷkaikaḷ pala piṟa ennāṭṭu emmoḻi nūlilum kāṇappaṭuvatillai. ituvē itaṟkuḷḷa taṉimai nalamum perumai nalamumākum.
iṉi, tamiḻnāṭṭut tamiḻmakkaḷuṭaiya taṉi nikaḻcci varalāṟukaṭkum, uṇmaikaṭkum, tamiḻar ūrkaṭkum taṉimaiyum toṉmaiyum uṇṭeṉpatu aṟiyātu, vaṭavarnāṭṭu vaṭavar vaṭamoḻikkaṇ toṭakkum tōṟṟamum nāṭi alamarum kīḻmai iṭaikkālattē eṅṅaṉamō uṇṭāyiṟṟu. tamiḻ
nāṭṭu mūtūr kaḷāṉa maturai, tillai. tiruvaṇṇāmalai, tiruvēṅkaṭam, tirukkāḷatti mutaliyavaṟṟiṉ toṉmai varalāṟum, tamiḻnāṭṭut tamiḻ makkaḷ nikaḻcci varalāṟukaḷāṉa cilappatikāram, tiruntoṇṭar purāṇam mutaliyavaṟṟukkum vaṭamoḻiyil toṭakkamum tōṟṟu vāyum kaṇṭuraippavar uḷarāyiṉar ‘aracamarattaip piṭitta caṉi, ataṉ kīḻ irunta aiṅkaraṉaiyum piṭitta teṉpatu pōla, mikap paḻantamiḻaratu toṉṉūlkaḷāṉa tirukkuṟaḷukkum tolkāppiyat tukkum “vaṭamoḻi mūlam” kaṇṭu, ilatākiya taṉāl poy yēṉum puṉaintu kūṟum puṉmai nāṭṭil uṇṭāki viṭṭatu. ataṉāl viḻuṅkappaṭṭōr palar, tiruvaḷḷuvar mutalāyi ṉōrkkup poy varalāṟu puṉaintatōṭu nillātu, tirukkuṟaḷ partturuhari, nīticatakam, pakavaṟ kītai, maṉumiruti, kauṭiliyar aruttacāttiram, cukkiranīti, kāmantakam mutaliya vaṭa nūlkaḷiṉ moḻi peyarppeṉṟum kūṟuvar.
tamiḻ vāḻviṉ taṉippayaṉāyt tōṉṟit tamiḻp pulamai ciṟantu tamiḻ niṉaivu tamiḻc ceyalkaḷait tamiḻuḷḷattiṟ kaṇṭuraitta tamiḻt taṉimakaṉārāṉa tiruvaḷḷuvar, taṅkālattu vaḻakkum ceyyuḷum ākiya iru perun tamiḻk kaṭalkaḷaiyum nuṇmāṇpulamai yeṉṉum mattiṉaik koṇṭukaṭain
teṭutta taṉipperuntamiḻamutam it taṇṭamiḻt  tirukkuṟaḷ eṉpatu yāvarum uḷḷakkiḻiyil uruvupeṟap poṟittuk koḷḷa vēṇṭiya uṇmaiyākum.
tirukkuṟaḷ eṉṉum ariya aṟanūl ulakiyalukkuriya peru nūlākum. ulakiyalil kūṟutaṟkuriya periya poruḷkaḷ pala vaṟṟiṉum, aṟam, poruḷ, iṉpam eṉṟa mūṉṟiṉum perumai mikka poruḷ vēṟē kiṭaiyātu. vīṭupēṟum mūṉṟaṉ ētuvākap peṟappaṭuvatēyaṉṟi, immūṉṟāṉumē eytap paṭuvatumākaliṉ, perumai yeṉpatu immūṉṟukkumē uriyateṉpatu tēṟṟam. ākavē, evvakaiyiṉum aṟa mutaliya mūṉṟumē perumaikku urimai koṇṭu niṟṟaliṉ am mūṉṟaṉaiyum viḷakkum nūlatu perumaikku vēṟē emmoḻi ennūlum iṇaiyākātākaliṉ, ituvē ellāpperumaikkum urittāyp perunūl eṉappaṭu vatāyiṟṟu.
perunūlkaḷai yāttaṟkup perumai mikka vaḻikaḷ pala uṇṭu. perumaiyaip peritāyk kāṭṭuvatai viṭa, ap perumai nalam ciṟitum kuṉṟātavakaiyil ciṟumaikkaṇ aṭakkip palarkkum teriyappaṇ ṇuvatu tāṉ pulavaratu cīrtta pulamaic ceyalākum. mēlum perumaip poruḷaip peruneṟipaṟṟi uṇarttaluṟiṉ,.  apperuneṟi tēṟi nuṉittuṇarum aṟivup perumaiyuṭaiyārkkē an nūl uriyatāyviṭum. nūṟporuḷ, periyōr ciṟiyōr aṉaivōrkkum potu
vātalāl, ciṟumaiyaṟiviṉarāṉa makkaḷ palarum aṟitaṟkākavē, pākkaḷuṭ ciṟukiyatum kuṟukiyatumāṉa kuṟaṭpākkaḷāl innūlai āciriyar tiruvaḷḷuvar yāttirukkiṉṟār.
7. nūṟpeyar
ivvāṟu, kuṟumpāvākiya kuṟaṭpāvāl, perum poruḷai yuṇartta vantavar, kuṟaḷ eṉṟē nūlukkum peyar kuṟittiruk kiṉṟaṉar. nūṟpeyar viḷaṅkap peyar kuṟittilar. ulakil nilavum perumporuḷai yellām oruvaḻiyāl iṉitu eḷitu viḷaṅkak kūṟuvatāl, nūṟporuḷai yuḷḷaṭakkiya potuppeyaroṉṟāl cuṭṭikkūṟal amaiyāmaipaṟṟi, karuviyākiya kuṟaṭpāviṉ peyarāl nūlaik kuṟittiruppatu nōkkattakkatu. itaṉkaṭ kūṟappaṭum aṟa mutaliya poruḷkaḷ oṉṟaṟkoṉṟu ētuvum payaṉumāy iyaintu pirippaṟa niṟṟalāl, immūṉṟaiyum tokuttuc cuṭṭavallatoru potuppeyar itaṟku eytātāyiṟṟu. aṟanūl eṉṟu peyar kuṟittāl, atu poruḷaiyum iṉpattaiyum coṉṉa māttirattē kēṭṭār maṉattuṭ kiḷarntu eḻuvikkum vaṉmaiyuṭaiyatā kātu; ēṉaipporuṇūl iṉpanūl eṉṟu kuṟitta vaḻiyum atuvē muṭivākum. iṉi oḻukkanūl eṉṟa vaḻi. atu, vaḻakku, taṇṭam eṉpaṉavaṟṟaiyum cuṭṭātākiṉṟatu; in nūṟkaṇ, vaḻakkum taṇṭamum āṅkāṅkup potuvakaiyil kūṟappaṭukiṉṟaṉa. ulakiyal nūl eṉṟakkāl. ataṉōṭiyaittuk kāṇappaṭum aṟivunūl eṉpataṉiṉ nīṅkit taṉimaiyuṟum; iḵtu aṟivu
nūlumātaliṉ, ciṟappuṭaiya oṉṟaṉ peyarāl varaintu kūṟuvatum kuṉṟak kūṟalākiṉṟatu. ivvāṟu ellāmāy allatumāy uḷḷa poruḷvakai ellāvaṟṟaiyum cuṭṭivarum innūṟkup poruḷiyalpu kāṭṭum peyar oṉṟu kiṭaittal aritātal kaṇṭē, āciriyar, kuṟaṭpāvākiya karuviyaṉālēyē kuṟaḷ eṉṉum peyarāl vaḻaṅkiṉareṉak koḷḷalvēṇṭum tiruvaḷḷuvaratu teyvap pulamaiyiṉ viḷaivākat tōṉṟiya ciṟappuppaṟṟic cāṉṟōr ikkuṟaQ lait tirukkuṟaḷ eṉṟu pārāṭṭu vārāyiṉar.
iṉi, it tirukkuṟaḷai aṟattuppāl. poruṭpāl, iṉpattuppāl (kāmattuppāl) eṉṟu mūvakaippaṭuttu, ovvoṉṟiṉaiyum cila pala atikāraṅkaḷāka vakuttu atikāram ovvoṉṟiṉaiyum pappattuk kuṟaṭpākkaḷāl kūṟiccelkiṉṟār. pappattāka niṟkum kuṟaḷkaḷum, eṭuttuk koṇṭa poruṭku ilakkaṇam kūṟuvatum, ētuvum payaṉum kūṟuvatum, poruḷiyal puraippatum, vitippatum, maṟuppatum, cila poruḷkaḷait tuṇai yeṉpatum, cilavaṟṟai aṇiyeṉpatum, cilavaṟṟaip paṭai yeṉpatum, muṟai ceyvatum, muṉpiṉ nōkki valiyuṟuttu vatum, nūlōr muṭipeṉpatum, cāṉṟōr mēlvaittuk kōṭaṟ pāṉmai kāṭṭuvatum, uvamai vakaiyāṟ cilavaṟṟai viḷakkaluṟṟu, poruḷaiyē uvamam ceyvatum, ēṉaiyavaṟṟai uvamam ceyvatum, urupu virittu uvamippatum, uvamaiyum poruḷum oruṅku vaittu uruvakam ceyvatum, oṭṭu, vañcap pukaḻcci mutaliya aṇiyuṟak kūṟuvatum,  viyappu, vekuḷi mutaliya meyp pāṭu tōṉṟak kūṟuvatumākiya pala vakaiyāl iyaṉṟuḷḷaṉa.
iṉi, aṟattuppāluḷ aṭaṅkiya atikāraṅkaḷuṭ cila
vaṟṟait tokuttup pāyirameṉavum, illaṟaviyal, tuṟaviṟaviyal eṉavum, poruṭpāl atikāraṅkaḷai vakuttu, araciyal, aṅkaviyal, oḻipiyal eṉavum, iṉpattuppāliṟ kāṇappaṭum atikāraṅkaḷai, kaḷaviyal kaṟpiyal eṉavum vakuttaṭakkik kūṟiṉar eṉpar. ivviyal vakuppukkaḷ oru neṟiyāka vaṉṟi vēṟu vēṟu vakaiyāka vakuttuk kūṟap paṭṭuḷḷaṉa eṉat tiruvaḷḷuvamālai terivikkiṟatu. maṇakkuṭavar uraiyil ivviyal vakuppu vēṟupaṭukiṟatu. itaṉāl, iyal vakuppu
maṭṭil āciriyar tiruvaḷḷuvaṉār koṇṭataṉṟu eṉpatu teḷiya viḷaṅkukiṟatu. atikāraṅ kaḷiṉ vaippumuṟai maṭṭil, parimē laḻakar, maṇakkuṭavar mutaliyōratu tokuppil vēṟupāṭiṉṟi oppa nilavuvatāl, ammuṟai, āciriyarē kaṇṭuraittateṉak koḷḷalām.
aṟivu, viḻaivu, ceyal eṉṟa mūṉṟum uyirkaṭkuriya paṇpukaḷākum. avaṟṟuḷ aṟivu poruṭ pēṟṟukkum, viḻaivu iṉpa nukarccikkum ceyal aṟañceytaṟkum payaṉākum. ippaṇpum payaṉum koṇṭu iṉpamē nāṭiniṟpatupaṟṟi, poruḷatikārattu iṉpattiṇaiyiṉ iyalpu kūṟappukunta tolkāppiyar “iṉpamum poruḷum aṟaṉum” eṉa muṟai ceytu kūṟiṉār. aṟattāṉ varuvatē iṉpam; poruḷilārkku ivvulakil iṉpamillai eṉa aṟamum poruḷum iṉpap pēṟṟukku ētuvātalait tiru vaḷḷuvar ceyalmuṟaiyiṟ kūṟukiṉṟār; ataṉāl aṟattuppālai mutaṟkaṇṇum, poruṭpālai iṭaiyilum, iṉpattuppālai muṭivilum muṟai ceytu
vaittu muppālākiya tirukkuṟaḷai uraittuḷḷār. iṉi, parimēlaḻakar, immai maṟumai vīṭu eṉṟa mūṉṟaiyum payappatu
paṟṟi aṟam mutalilum, immai maṟumai eṉṟa iraṇṭaiyum payappatupaṟṟip poruḷ iṭaiyilum immai yoṉṟē payappatupaṟṟi iṉpam iṟutiyilum vaikkap paṭṭaṉa eṉpar.
8. araṅkēṟṟam
iṉi, it tirukkuṟaḷ nūl maturaiyil irunta caṅkattil araṅkēṟṟappaṭṭateṉṟum, akkālattē innūlai ēṟka maṟutta caṅkappulavar, ittirukkuṟaṭku iṭantarumukattāl caṅkap
palakaiyāl teruṭṭap paṭṭaṉa reṉṟum kūṟappaṭum ceytiyum piṟkālattōr puṉaintu viṭṭa poyyuraiyē. nallataṉ nalattai uḷḷavāṟu kāṇum uravōrē pulavareṉap paṭuvarākai yāl, avar kūṭiya pēravaiyil tiruvaḷḷuvar mutaṟkaṇ puṟakkaṇikkap paṭṭāreṉṟaṟku accaṅkac cāṉṟōr ceytuḷḷa pāṭṭukkaḷiṉ tokutikaḷākak kāṇappaṭum tokai nūlkaḷ ātaravu taruvaṉavāka illai.
9. iṟutinilai
muppāl vaḻaṅki muḻutulakum toḻutēttum mūvāp pukaḻpeṟṟa mutaṟ pulavarāṉa tiruvaḷḷuvaṉār, piṉpu, ēlēlaciṅkar eṉṉum celvar toṭarpu peṟṟu maṉaiyaṟam purintuvantaṉar eṉṟa ceytipotuvākak kūṟappaṭukiṟatu. avaṉ ivarpāl māṇavaṉāy aṟaṅkēṭṭāṉ eṉpārum, ivar avaṉpāl nūlpeṟṟu necavuttoḻil purintār eṉpārum pala tiṟattar uḷar. ivai ettuṇai yuṇmaiyāṉavai eṉpatu aṟiyak
kūṭāta tāyiṉum, iruvarkkum toṭarpu iruntateṉak koḷvatil kuṟṟamillai tiruvaḷḷuvarkku makkaṭpēṟu uṇṭō illaiyō eṉṟu kūṟattakka vakaiyil avar varalāṟu viḷakka miṉṟiyuḷatu. tirukkuṟaḷai nōkkumiṭattu avarkku makkaṭpēṟu uṇṭeṉat teḷitaṟku iṭamirukkiṟatu. avarkaḷ tiruvaḷḷuvarpōl poṉ ṟāppukaḻ peṟum pēṟu ilarāka irukkalām.
tiruvaḷḷuvar viṇpukuvataṟku muṉpē avar maṉaiviyār tam pūtavuṭampu nīttaṉareṉa avar varalāṟu kūṟukiṟatu. avar pirivāṟṟātu vaḷḷuvar varuntip pāṭiyatākap pāṭṭoṉṟum kāṭṭappaṭukiṟatu. ciṉṉāḷ kaḻittut tiruvaḷḷuvarum iṟaivaṉ tiruvaṭi cērntā reṉṟum, avaruṭaiya māṇavarākiya ēlēla ciṅkarē iṟutikkaṭaṉ āṟṟiṉāreṉṟum kūṟukiṉṟaṉar. yātōr āṭamparamumiṉṟi mikka eḷiya nilaiyil tiruvaḷḷuvar tamatu vāḻkkaiyai naṭatti iṟaivaṉ tiruvaṭi nīḻaleytiṉāreṉṟu koḷvatē ivaratu iṟutipaṟṟik kūṟappaṭum nikaḻccikaḷiṉ uṇmaik karuttākum.
10. piṟa nūlkaḷ
‘tūṅkiṉavaṉ tuṭaiyil tirittatu kayiṟu’ eṉṟāṟ pōla, tiruvaḷḷuvarpērāl ñāṉaveṭṭiyāṉ, tiruvaḷḷuvar ñāṉam eṉpaṉa mutaliya ciṟu nūlkaḷ āṅkāṅkuk kāṇappaṭukiṉṟaṉa. avaṟṟiṟkum tirukkuṟaṭkum ettuṇaip poruntāmai
yuṇṭō, attuṇaip poruntāmai avarkkum an nūl
kaḷukkum uṇṭeṉpatu kaṇṭu aṟivuṭaiyulakam amaintoḻuku
kiṉṟatu vāḻka tiruvaḷḷuvar! vāḻka tirukkuṟaḷ!!
vāḻka!
“vaḷḷuvaṉ taṉṉai yulakiṉukkē tantu
  vāṉpukaḻ koṇṭa tamiḻ nāṭu”


AN INTRODUCTION TO 
THE STUDY OF 
THIRUVALLUVAR
Foreword
T.Sundaram Chettiar, B.Com.,
District Governer,
Lions International Dist, 304 (South) Madurai.

It will not be an exaggeration if we say that, of all the great poets,philosophers and thinkers of the ancient as well as modern times,  Thiruvalluvar stands foremost. But it is a matter for regret that his immortal work, Thirukkural, perhaps the greatest and noblest lliterary creation of the human intellect ever produced, is not so much known to the outside world as those of Homer or Virgil, Plato or Aristotle, Dante or Goethe and Shakespeare or Milton.
	Even in India, beyond the boundaries of Tamil Nad, the knowledge and study of Thirukkural is deplorably meagre and though it far exceeds in its grand and noble conception of the philosophy of life, both material and spiritual, and in its scientific treatment of the four branches, viz., Aram, Porul, Inbham and Veedu, it has not become as much popular as Ghita or Upanishads.
	The reason for this sorry state of affairs is not far to seek.  Despite the fact that Thirukkural, according to one of its titles - Podhumarai - is connon to all nations, all religions and acceptable for all times, it still remains a sealed book and a forbidden fruit to many of the Indians and to the foreigner.  We have to blame ourselves for this because, while people of other countries, why even our brethren like Bengalis, are propagating their language and literature throughout the world, we are vainly indulging in our glorious past.
Yet it is gratifying to note that attempts are now being made by Tamil scholars, educationsl and other cultural bodies to spread the knowledge of Thirukkural among the peoples of the world and it is being translated in different languages by competent authors.  I am particularly  glad that one of such attempt by my esteemed and learned friend Professor Avvai S.Duraiswami Pillai has resulted in the form of a paper entitled as “An Introduction To The Study of Thiruvalluvar” which has been now brought out in a book form with a view to help the non-Tamils to have access to the greatest, most valuable and inexhaustible treasure - Thirukkural.  While congratulating Professor Avvai S.Duraiswami Pillai of his achievement, I wish that he and other Scholars of Tamil should offer many more such treatises to non-Tamils especially to Europeans and Americans.
T.SUNDARAM.

AN INTRODUCTION TO THE STUDY OF  THIRUVALLUVAR
Mr. President, Ladies and Gentlemen,
I have great pleasure in expressing at the outset my grateful thanks for the kind opportunity, you, the Lions of Madurai, have given me to address you on Thiruvalluvar.  This opportunity is noteworthy for the reason that our Union Government has recognised the greatness of Thiruvalluvar and has issued Postal Stamps with his image.
The present trend of thiking among the educated is quite different from that of those in my younger days; the outlook of our young men now is broad and international.  It looks upon man as man; his colour, caste and creed are ignored.  The uplift of man, as Tolstoy puts it, lies in the endeavour to raise the super element of human aspect and curb and control “the animal man” in him.
It is therefore proper and legitimate the the Lions club of Madurai takes up for its study Thiruvalluvar whose object and aim of study centre in and around the human society, Valluvar, you know, lived in Mdurai when the Egyptian and Athenian civilizations were at zenith.  Great thinkers and philosophers like Plato, Aristotle and a host of others were shining bright and influencing the human society to a greater extent.
Ancient Grecian writers inform us of the import of Tugi from Indies; Thiruvalluvar also points out the export of the same tugi in one of his couplets.
1.	pīlipey cākāṭum acciṟum appaṇṭam
   	cāla mikuttup peyiṉ
Our Bharat was not wanting in thinkers like Thirualluvar in those days.  There were Bodhayana, Apasthamba.  Asvalayana, Koutilya and Parasara, besides Manu, the law giver.  But the mind of these thinkers was so saturated with communal feelings called Varnashrama Dharma that they could not extricate themselves from bias.  They thought and did every thing in terms of caste and community.  Even the author of the famous  Bhagavat Gita, who devotes a separate chapter to the castes and the respective communal duties and Birthru Hari, the author of Neethi Sathagam,  are not free from this mental distemper.
That is why Prof. Sundaram Pillai went to the extent of saying.
2. 	vaḷḷuvarcey tirukkuṟaḷai maṟuvaṟa naṉku 				uṇarntōrkaḷ
 	uḷḷuvarō maṉuvāti orukulattukku orunīti
i.e if anybody studies Thirukkural in detail he will never like the code of Manu and Smirities which mete out justice on the basis of caste and community.
From these we can see that Thiruvalluvar is the only great thinker superior to those law givers and moralists mentioned before and his views are in tune with the modern sociological trends which tend to a formation of a casteless and classless community, free from all types of distinctions by virtue of birth or practice. Sublimation of life by following the natural rules of conduct is the sole aim of Tirukkural and it does not place any religious or political faith or thought, as the be-all and the end-all of life.  It is in pursuance of this aim that Valluvar takes the whole human society into consideration and wants to establish it on the bed-rock of moral virtue.  The unit of society according to him consists of husband and wife.   If the man and woman of a family - unit are well qualified to lead a virtuous life, the whole of an ideal society would enjoy permanent peace and pleasure.
In the introductory chapters of Tirukkural,  human society is viewed both materially and morally.  In the first chapter.  Thiruvalluvar explains the importance of prayer to God as the only solution for the scares and anxieties and pangs of birth and death, the ambitious longing for wealth and pleasure And the last, if not the least,  the innate ingnorance which is responsible for all the deeds, good or bad,
The drama of human life is to be enacted at the face of the earth.  The material aspect of  life  rests upon the food-stuff, which is the immediate product of the rain; but just like the grace of God,  rain is also beyond the control of man. 
The existence of worldly life is  dependent upon the rain; it is the only thing responsible to ensure living  for all beings. Thiruvalluvar has thought very minutely of the importance  of  the rain and declares that even though the earth is surrounded by expansive seas and oceans;   it will be as useless as the poet says, “ Water water everywhere, not a drop to drink.’’   Modern science can provide us with distilled water when our water cans go dry in the midst of the sea, but this artifical distilled water is tasteless and hence incapable of quenching our thirst; that is why thiruvalluvar says that even the watery oceans will shrink in their importance, if there is no rain at all.
3. 	neṭuṅkaṭalum taṉṉīrmai kuṉṟum taṭintueḻili
   	tāṉnalkā tāki viṭiṉ
Man maintains his power of elevating his human spirit to super human and divine level simply because his physical actvities are crowned with pleasure and plenty on account of the rain.  Consequently  penance and patience help him to raise his spiritual existence up and above ordinary human level.
4. 	tāṉam tavamiraṇṭum taṅkā viyaṉulakam
  	vāṉam vaḻaṅkā teṉiṉ.
Even when the human society, by the grace of Lord God and the heavenly rain, is provided with peace and plenty, its worldly social life has to be governed by the most experienced and wise men of keen intellect and broad spiritual outlook.   They are gifted with keen insight so that with the philosophic process of analysis and synthesis they are able to analyse the universe into five and more elements mentally and appreciate psychologically the inner working of each element.  This is put in a nutshell as.
5. 	cuvai yoḷi ūṟuōcai nāṟṟameṉṟaintiṉ
	vakaiterivāṉ kaṇṇatē ulaku.
Having lived their life most profitably both for others and for themselves, they take to asectic life and devote their time to give pieces of advice to the experienced and guide the inexperienced. 
The ascetic life they had adopted gives ample opportunities for introspection and self negation and ripens their mind to render all possible help to their fellow beings.  That is why their actions are considered to be the greatest which no other human being can do.  This our Valluvar says:
6. 	ceyaṟkariya ceyvār periyar ciṟiyar
   	ceyaṟkariya ceykalā tār.
They are embodiments of love and sympathy.  They are called antaṇar!(Andhanar)  because their love is towards the welfare of all living beings.  As such, they become the accredited leaders of the human society.  Every word they utter is held as law for human conduct.   Their words and ideas of virtues, morals, laws and regulations are incorporated in this word kural.
In the last chapter of the Introductory part.  Thiruvalluvar gives the general scope of the aṟam (Aram) which he wants to present to us in the following chapters.
Aram in Tamil is a very comprehensive term and it includes all virtues, morals, laws both individual and social and regulations of conduct both in the private and public life.  The first and foremost object of any individual is to attain glory and obtain wealth.  He desires that his life graph must always be straight and on the positive side i.e. plus side, and so Thiruvalluvar says to him that this is possible and attainable if Aram is adhered to strictly and followed most faithfully.
Man’s actions, good or bad, are first conceived in the mind and then put into action.  Aram can be worth doing only when it is conceived in the mind which is pure.  Therefore the first requisite he enjoins on us is to be pure in mind.
7.	maṉattukkaṇ mācuila ṉātal aṉaittu aṟaṉ
 	ākula nīra piṟa.
In putting this Aram into action one must be careful in eschewing uncharitable envy, illegitimate desire, unscrupulous anger and mischievous offensive speech.  The purpose of man’s life on earth is to attain pleasure;  no power or pelf is capable of bringing lasting peace and pleasure; history tells us in its true verasity, that sceptre and  crown have tumbled down and rich diadems have been thrown away rejected on the ground that they were incapable of yielding happiness with peace.  Man’s burning desire is to attain unalloyed, untroubled and everlasting pleasure and peace.  Even this is not impossible provided yo are an ardent devotee of Aram; Aram is the only source of that everlasting pleasure and nothing else.
8.	aṟattāṉ varuvatē iṉpammaṟṟu ellām 
  	puṟatta pukaḻum ila.    
PART - I
Gentlemen.
	Having introduced Aram in this way Thiruvalluvar begins to enlarge his subject of Aram and exhorts the human society to follow it.
In the beginning he begins with the house hold life because according to Valluvar and his predecessors as we have already seen family is the unit of the human society.  But our modern politicians advocate individuals as the unit of the soceity and they base all the Governmental activities taking the individual male or female as the unit.  According to our traditions and culture family-unit is indivisable and sacred.  Splitting family into individuals has torpeded peace and tranquility at home.  This reduces the house to a temporary lodging for the male and the female individuals.  There is another school of thought professing the village community as the unit of the human society.  Here, the unit is heterogeneous in its structure and the active are absent on account os their exodus to urban areas.  Thus we can see how realistic our Valluvar is and true to our culture; he gives a lead to the entire human community.
The domestic life of each family or the unit of the human society is replete with duties and services to be rendered to the community at large.   Human life on earth is always interdependent; physical, economical, social and intellectual inder-dependance is the summum bonum of life.  With this in his view, Valluvar says that every house-holder must be of help,  assistance, and guidance to his fellowmen besides being a mainstay of the ascetics, poor and needy, and those who are too old and feeble to fend for themselves.  Ancestral worship, devotion to duty, hospitality to the guests and timely help to relatives are the general duties imposed upon each householder.  In discharging these duties, the male and female of the family-unit must on no account forget to preserve their person by attending to its needs and comforts.  It is the human body which is the most indispensable instrument of carrying out the various Arams; the essence of existence.  The wife of the house-holder is called the life-partner and she occupies the supreme position of the family in its internal affairs.  In the days of Valluvar their union was not separable by any law or regulation.  No government had the right to cut asunder their partnership in life.  The government of the family was entirely in the hands of the wife and her family budget never ran into deficit.  She was the home and finance minister to the government of the family.
	Every duty of the family is discharged in consanance with the dignity of the house.  And the domestic expenditure is in proportion to its income.  The name and fame of the family rests entirely in the virtuous conduct of the wife.  Among the most glorious things on earth, nothing is superior to a woman who upholds her chastity with firmness of mind.
9.   peṇṇiṟ peruntakka yāvuḷa kaṟpeṉṉum
     tiṇmaiyuṇ ṭākap peṟiṉ
To her the loving husband is the Lord God, and bowing low at his feet she controls the entire elements of nature, and comands them to execute her wish.  Valluvar says that rains will pour down if she desires.  She is vigilent in guarding herself and her chastity, always attentive to her husband’s comfort, ever anxious to safeguard the tradition and dignity of the family and unfailing in the discharge of her domestic duties.  It is her sincere conviction that her husband, her life partner, attains everlasting glory and bliss. She will, in turn, without any doubt be deified in the world where gods live.
10.   peṟṟāṉ peṟiṟpeṟuvar peṇṭir peruñciṟappup 
       puttēḷir vāḻum ulaku.
	She therefore directs her thought, word and deed towards raising the right reputation of her husband in the paths of domestic virtue.
Any lacking in this aspect of her life will not only drive the whole family to docks but her poor husband will lose his manly appearance, lion-like walk, before his scorning foes.
11.   pukaḻpurinta illilōrkku illai ikaḻvārmuṉ
      ēṟupōl pīṭu naṭai.
From these we come to the conclusion that a virtuous wife is a real blessing to a house.  But this is not sufficient.  She must  bring forth brilliant children.  They are fine ornaments to the household.  She is not only responsible for the prevalence of peace and pleasure but should beautify the household life by bringing up good children.  It must be clearly understood that procreating children is not the sole purpose ot the family life; according to Valluvar, Tholkappiar and other ancient Tamil saints, the childen were true ornaments as well as wealth to the family and to that effect they were brought up.
Of all the valuable acquisitions a husband and wife of a family can have, nothing is greater than begetting intelligent children.  Sons and daughters of unimpeachable character will enable their parents to enjoy all pleasure not only in this life but in the seven-fold births they may take in future and that is because great thinkers of the past declared one’s children as one’s fortune.  The wishes and the best thing that a father can do to his children is to educate them to such an extent as would elevate them to the forefront of the councils of the wise.
The parents feel that, by the education they give, if their sons and daughters possess for higher degree of knowledge than themselves, it would do immense good to all beings at large.  When the society recognises the greatness of her son and offers him the foremost of its place, the mother’s heart is filled with pride and happiness exceedingly greater than what she enjoyed when she gave birth to him.  Having shown the true extent of the parental care and love towards the intellectual growth of the son, Valluvar exhorts the son that the best requittal that a greatful son could offer to his father is to make the wise men praise the parent for presenting so noble and virtuous a son for the benefit of the human society.  With this chapter Valluvar completes the structure of the unit of the human society.
These family-units are bound together by the sacred bond of genuine love; this love is spontaneous and nothing can obstruct it from its flow.  From love springs affection to all and that in turn results in real friendship with others
12.aṉpuīṉum ārva muṭaimai atuīṉum
	naṇpueṉṉum nāṭāc ciṟappu!
This genuine, spontaneous flow of love not only encourages virtue but is of utmost importance to avoid and remove evil from the minds of the wicked and cruel.  The very existence of the living beings is due to this love; but for  this love, the whole universe will be devoid of entire beings and it will be full of skin-clad bones.  We thus understand that love is nothing but a binding force pulling all the members together to form a solid human entity called family.  This love bound family comes in contact with the other units and by mutual love, these units of the human society are linked and knit together.  Thus the life of the human beings becomes the movement of love, affection and friendship
In 17 chapters, Valluvar deals with the positive and negative characteristics of the working of the family units along with the whole human society.  Instead of entering into the details, let me confine myself to the presentation of the general scope for your thought at present.
Reception and cordial entertainment of guests and other fellowmen, utterance of kind and sincerely sweet words, gratefulness to people who are of help, assistance and guidance and sense of uprightness and equity in transactions are the main positive elements for success in family life Of course, experience of our life on earth tell us that the course is not smooth.  There are some negative forces arising out of our movement in the society at large.  Failure to control our mind and speech, covetuousness, lack of forbearance and fortitude, evil tendency of enjoying others’ possessions and back-biting, indulgence in frivolous and vain talks are the most important negative characteristics which impede the pursuit of the house-holder to achieve his object.  In order to overcome these anti-social negative elements, Valluvar points out the importance of the generosity of mind, liberality of help to others without any thought of recompense, feeding the hungry and helping the poor and needy.
The ultimate fruit of a life devoted to the various duties already enunciated is earthly name and fair renown; otherwise life is not worth living.  As regards the reception of people, Valluvar says that the love that has hitherto been confined within the family - husband, wife and child - begins to manifest itself when a guest arrives to the family.  No matter whether the guest is a stranger or not, he is received with courtesy and affection.  The householder thinks that the sole object of running a family is to receive and serve others whether they are already known or otherwise.
13. iruntu ōmpi ilvāḻva tellām viruntōmpi 		    vēḷāṇmai ceytaṟ poruṭṭu
In order to achieve this object every individual must speak sweet and kind words.  Only such words are declared sweet which come out from  righteous persons with heart full of love and speech completely guileless.
14. iṉcolāl īram aḷaiip paṭiṟuilavām
	cemporuḷ kaṇṭārvāyc col
Speaking always kind and pleasant words born of humility, not only serves as an ornament but protectes one from falling a prey to indigence which produces misery.
15. tuṉpuṟūum tuvvāmai illākum yārmaṭṭum
	iṉpuṟūum  iṉcola varkku.
It helps every man to maintain friendly relation with all.  The next allied Aram that brings an individual into the fold of the society is his gratefulness to the help and assistance he receives from others.  As every human being is interdependent, helping and being helped by others are not infrequent.  Some of the helps received might have been done without any expectation of return, some may be in times of need and some might have been without any thought of its high value.  Even when the help done is as small as millet’s seed, wise men who realise the fruits of the act, will only think of the benefit derived by such help and be grateful.
